Here the covariant derivatives
3
3

3

From Eq. (16.34) in Ref.
[
8
]
we find that for a general superconformal chiral multiplet
(z^{I},P_{L}\chi^{I},F^{I})
with Weyl/chiral weights
(w,c=w)
and gauge symmetries with Killing vector fields
k_{A}^{I}
, the full superconformal covariant derivatives
\mathcal{D}_{a}
are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{a}z^{I}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{\mu}_{a}\Big[(\partial_{\mu}-wb_{\mu}-wiA_{\mu})z^{I}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\bar{\psi}_{\mu}\chi^{I}-A^{A}_{\mu}k_{A}^{I}\Big],

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{a}P_{L}\chi^{I}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{\mu}_{a}P_{L}\bigg[\left(\partial_{\mu}+\frac{1}{4}\omega_{\mu}^{ab}\gamma_{ab}-(w+1/2)b_{\mu}+(w-3/2)iA_{\mu}\right)\chi^{I}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}z^{I}+F^{I})\psi_{\mu}

\displaystyle-\sqrt{2}wz^{I}\phi_{\mu}-A^{A}_{\mu}\chi^{J}\partial_{J}k_{A}^{I}\bigg].

of chiral multiplets of matter with the weights
(0,0)
are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{a}z^{I}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{\mu}_{a}\Big[\partial_{\mu}z^{I}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\bar{\psi}_{\mu}\chi^{I}\Big],

(7.28)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{a}P_{L}\chi^{I}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{\mu}_{a}P_{L}\bigg[\left(\partial_{\mu}+\frac{1}{4}\omega_{\mu}^{ab}\gamma_{ab}-\frac{1}{2}b_{\mu}-\frac{3}{2}iA_{\mu}\right)\chi^{I}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}z^{I}+F^{I})\psi_{\mu}\bigg].

Note that the only bosonic contribution to
\mathcal{D}_{K}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{K}|_{\textrm{boson}}=2K_{I\bar{J}}\left(-\partial_{\mu}z^{I}\partial^{\mu}\bar{z}^{\bar{J}}+F^{I}\bar{F}^{\bar{J}}\right)\equiv\tilde{\mathcal{F}}

(7.30)

and we especially denote this by
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}
. We also note that the
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}
is positive definite up to terms containing spatial gradients; so, for small spatial gradients,

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{K}|_{\textrm{boson}}\equiv\tilde{\mathcal{F}}\sim 2K_{I\bar{J}}\left(\dot{z}^{I}\dot{\bar{z}}^{\bar{J}}+F^{I}\bar{F}^{\bar{J}}\right)>0.

(7.31)

7.2.2
Compensator multiplet

Chiral compensators

with the Weyl/chiral weights (1,1) are defined as follows. The chiral supermultiplets

(7.122)

\displaystyle\left(X^{W},P_{L}\chi^{W},F^{W}\right),

7.3
Liberated Term

Then, the lowest component of the final composite real multiplet is

\displaystyle N

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{2}\frac{w^{2}\bar{w}^{2}}{T(\bar{w}^{2})\bar{T}(w^{2})}\mathcal{U}=\Upsilon^{-2}\dfrac{\mathcal{W}^{2}(K)\bar{\mathcal{W}}^{2}(K)}{T(w^{2})\bar{T}(\bar{w}^{2})}\mathcal{U}

(7.127)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(X^{0}\bar{X}^{0}e^{-K(z^{I},\bar{z}^{\bar{I}})/3})^{-2}X^{W}\bar{X}^{\bar{W}}(X^{T})^{-1}(\bar{X}^{\bar{T}})^{-1}\mathcal{U}(z^{I},\bar{z}^{\bar{I}}).

Then, since
N
is now expressed as a function of superconformal chiral multiplets, we can represent the full off-shell expression of the superconformal Lagrangian of the liberated
\mathcal{N}=1
supergravity via Eq. (17.19) of Freedman and Van Proeyen
[
fvp
]
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{NEW}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle[\mathbb{N}]_{D}e^{-1}

(7.128)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{D}_{N}-\frac{1}{4}\bar{\psi}\cdot\gamma i\gamma_{*}\Lambda_{N}-\frac{1}{6}\mathcal{C}_{N}R(\omega)+\frac{1}{12}\left(\mathcal{C}_{N}\bar{\psi}_{\mu}\gamma^{\mu\rho\sigma}-i\bar{\mathcal{Z}}_{N}\gamma^{\rho\sigma}\gamma_{*}\right)R_{\rho\sigma}^{\prime}(Q)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{8}\varepsilon^{abcd}\bar{\psi}_{a}\gamma_{b}\psi_{c}(\mathcal{B}_{d}^{N}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\psi}_{d}\mathcal{Z}_{N}),

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle N_{i\bar{j}}\bigg(-\mathcal{D}_{\mu}X^{i}\mathcal{D}^{\mu}\bar{X}^{\bar{j}}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\chi}^{i}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\chi^{\bar{j}}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\chi}^{\bar{j}}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\chi^{i}+F^{i}\bar{F}^{\bar{j}}\bigg)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\bigg[N_{ij\bar{k}}\Big(-\bar{\chi}^{i}\chi^{j}\bar{F}^{\bar{k}}+\bar{\chi}^{i}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}X^{j})\chi^{\bar{k}}\Big)+h.c.\bigg]+\frac{1}{4}N_{ij\bar{k}\bar{l}}\bar{\chi}^{i}\chi^{j}\bar{\chi}^{\bar{k}}\chi^{\bar{l}}

\displaystyle+\bigg[\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}}\bar{\psi}\cdot\gamma\bigg(N_{i\bar{j}}F^{i}\chi^{\bar{j}}-N_{i\bar{j}}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{X}^{\bar{j}}\chi^{i}-\frac{1}{2}N_{ij\bar{k}}\chi^{\bar{k}}\bar{\chi}^{i}\chi^{j}\bigg)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{8}i\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\bar{\psi}_{\mu}\gamma_{\nu}\psi_{\rho}\bigg(N_{i}\mathcal{D}_{\sigma}X^{i}+\frac{1}{2}N_{i\bar{j}}\bar{\chi}^{i}\gamma_{\sigma}\chi^{\bar{j}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}N_{i}\bar{\psi}_{\sigma}\chi^{i}\bigg)+h.c.\bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{6}N\left(-R(\omega)+\frac{1}{2}\bar{\psi}_{\mu}\gamma^{\mu\nu\rho}R^{\prime}_{\nu\rho}(Q)\right)-\frac{1}{6\sqrt{2}}\left(N_{i}\bar{\chi}^{i}+N_{\bar{i}}\bar{\chi}^{\bar{i}}\right)\gamma^{\mu\nu}R^{\prime}_{\mu\nu}(Q),

where
i,j=0,I,W,T
and
\bar{j},\bar{k},\bar{l}=\bar{0},\bar{I},\bar{W},\bar{T}
and

\displaystyle R_{\mu\nu ab}(\omega)\equiv\partial_{\mu}\omega_{\nu ab}-\partial_{\nu}\omega_{\mu ab}+\omega_{\mu ac}\omega_{\nu~b}^{~c}-\omega_{\nu ac}\omega_{\mu~b}^{~c},

(7.130)

\displaystyle R_{\mu\nu}^{\prime}(Q)\equiv 2\left(\partial_{[\mu}+\frac{1}{2}b_{[\mu}-\frac{3}{2}A_{[\mu}\gamma_{*}+\frac{1}{4}\omega_{[\mu}^{ab}(e,b,\psi)\gamma_{ab}\right)\psi_{\nu]},

(7.131)

\displaystyle\omega_{\mu}^{ab}(e,b,\psi)=\omega_{\mu}^{ab}(e,b)+\frac{1}{2}\psi_{\mu}\gamma^{[a}\psi^{b]}+\frac{1}{4}\bar{\psi}^{a}\gamma_{\mu}\psi^{b}.

(7.132)

7.3.1
Bosonic lagrangians

The bosonic contribution to the scalar potential can be found from the term
\mathcal{D}_{N}
. Using the above results and Eq. (
), we find the equivalent bosonic contribution to the scalar potential as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{B}\supset N_{i\bar{j}}F^{i}\bar{F}^{\bar{j}}\sim N_{W\bar{W}}F^{W}\bar{F}^{\bar{W}}=\Upsilon^{-2}\frac{1}{\mathcal{C}_{T}\mathcal{C}_{\bar{T}}}\mathcal{U}F^{W}\bar{F}^{\bar{W}}.

(7.133)

Since
\mathcal{C}_{T}=-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{K}_{\bar{w}}\sim-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathcal{K}_{\bar{W}}}{\Upsilon^{2}}=-\frac{1}{2}\frac{(-2\bar{F}^{\bar{W}})}{\Upsilon^{2}}=\frac{\bar{F}^{\bar{W}}}{\Upsilon^{2}}
and
\mathcal{C}_{\bar{T}}=\frac{F^{W}}{\Upsilon^{2}}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{B}\supset\Upsilon^{-2}\frac{\Upsilon^{2}\Upsilon^{2}}{\bar{F}^{\bar{W}}F^{W}}\mathcal{U}F^{W}\bar{F}^{\bar{W}}=\Upsilon^{2}\mathcal{U}.

(7.134)

In the superconformal gauge
\Upsilon=1
, we get

\displaystyle V_{NEW}=\mathcal{U}.

(7.135)

7.3.2
Fermionic lagrangians

In this section, we investigate fermionic terms in the liberated
\mathcal{N}=1
supergravity Lagrangian. We focus in particular
on the matter-coupling and on the most divergent fermionic terms, in order to explore interesting interactions and check the limits
of validity of the liberated
\mathcal{N}=1
supergravity as an effective theory.

First of all, let us recall that the final composite multiplet
N
in terms of the superconformal chiral multiplets
\{S_{0},z^{I},W\equiv\mathcal{W}^{2}(K),T\equiv T(\bar{w}^{2})\}
are:

\displaystyle N=\left(s_{0}s_{0}^{*}e^{-K(z^{I},\bar{z}^{\bar{I}})/3}\right)^{-2}\dfrac{W\bar{W}}{T\bar{T}}\mathcal{U}(z^{I},\bar{z}^{\bar{I}}).

(7.136)

We we also denote their lowest components as
W\equiv\bar{\Lambda}_{K}P_{L}\Lambda_{K}
,
\bar{W}\equiv\bar{\Lambda}_{K}P_{R}\Lambda_{K}
,
T\equiv\mathcal{C}_{T}
, and
\bar{T}\equiv\mathcal{C}_{\bar{T}}
. Note that the final composite multiplet consists of the four superconformal chiral multiplets only. Remember that
\mathcal{C}_{T}\sim\dfrac{\bar{F}^{\bar{W}}}{\Upsilon^{2}}
and
F^{W}\propto\mathcal{D}_{K}^{2}|_{\textrm{boson}}\equiv\tilde{\mathcal{F}}^{2}
.

Generically, the matter couplings are found from the following contributions:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{F}^{\textrm{matter}}|_{\psi=0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle N_{i\bar{j}}\bigg(-\mathcal{D}_{\mu}X^{i}\mathcal{D}^{\mu}\bar{X}^{\bar{j}}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\chi}^{i}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\chi^{\bar{j}}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\chi}^{\bar{j}}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\chi^{i}+F^{i}\bar{F}^{\bar{j}}\bigg)-\frac{N}{6}R(\omega)|_{\psi=0}

(7.137)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\bigg[N_{ij\bar{k}}\Big(-\bar{\chi}^{i}\chi^{j}\bar{F}^{\bar{k}}+\bar{\chi}^{i}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}X^{j})\chi^{\bar{k}}\Big)+h.c.\bigg]+\frac{1}{4}N_{ij\bar{k}\bar{l}}\bar{\chi}^{i}\chi^{j}\bar{\chi}^{\bar{k}}\chi^{\bar{l}}\bigg|_{\psi=0},

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{F1}+\mathcal{L}_{F2}+\bar{\mathcal{L}}_{F2}+\mathcal{L}_{F3}+(\mathcal{L}_{F4}+\mathcal{L}_{F5}+h.c.)+\mathcal{L}_{F6}+\mathcal{L}_{F7}

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\mu}X^{i}|_{\psi=0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\partial_{\mu}-w_{i}b_{\mu}-w_{i}A_{\mu})X^{i},

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\mu}P_{L}\chi^{i}|_{\psi=0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\partial_{\mu}+\frac{1}{4}\omega_{\mu}^{ab}\gamma_{ab}-(w_{i}+1/2)b_{\mu}+(w_{i}-3/2)iA_{\mu}\right)P_{L}\chi^{i}-\sqrt{2}w_{i}X^{i}P_{L}\phi_{\mu}.

Note that the matter couplings of fermions can be classified into seven types:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{F1}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle-N_{i\bar{j}}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}X^{i}\mathcal{D}^{\mu}\bar{X}^{\bar{j}}\Big|_{\psi=0},

(7.138)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{F2}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}N_{i\bar{j}}\bar{\chi}^{i}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\chi^{\bar{j}}\Big|_{\psi=0},

(7.139)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{F3}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle-N_{i\bar{j}}F^{i}\bar{F}^{\bar{j}}\Big|_{\psi=0},

(7.140)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{F4}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}N_{ij\bar{k}}\bar{\chi}^{i}\chi^{j}\bar{F}^{\bar{k}}\Big|_{\psi=0},

(7.141)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{F5}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}N_{ij\bar{k}}\bar{\chi}^{i}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}X^{j})\chi^{\bar{k}}\Big|_{\psi=0},

(7.142)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{F6}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}N_{ij\bar{k}\bar{l}}\bar{\chi}^{i}\chi^{j}\bar{\chi}^{\bar{k}}\chi^{\bar{l}}\Big|_{\psi=0},

(7.143)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{F7}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle-\frac{N}{6}R(\omega)|_{\psi=0}.

(7.144)

The derivatives of the
N
are given in general by

\displaystyle N^{(r=q+p+m+k)}_{q,p,m,k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\partial_{0}^{q}\partial_{W}^{p}\partial_{T}^{m}\partial_{I}^{k}N)

(7.145)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg[(\partial_{0}^{q}\partial_{I}^{(k-n)}\Upsilon^{-2})_{(N)}(\Upsilon^{2})_{(\mathcal{C}_{T})}(\Upsilon^{2})_{(\mathcal{C}_{\bar{T}})}

\displaystyle\times(\Upsilon^{2m_{1}})_{(\partial_{T}^{m_{1}}\mathcal{C}_{T})}(\Upsilon^{2m_{2}})_{(\partial_{\bar{T}}^{m_{2}}\mathcal{C}_{\bar{T}})}(W)^{1-p_{1}}(\bar{W})^{1-p_{2}}\mathcal{U}^{(n)}\bigg]

\displaystyle/\bigg[(\tilde{\mathcal{F}}^{2})_{(\mathcal{C}_{T})}(\tilde{\mathcal{F}}^{2})_{(\mathcal{C}_{\bar{T}})}(\tilde{\mathcal{F}}^{2m_{1}})_{(\partial_{T}^{m_{1}}\mathcal{C}_{T})}(\tilde{\mathcal{F}}^{2m_{2}})_{(\partial_{\bar{T}}^{m_{2}}\mathcal{C}_{\bar{T}})}\bigg].

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\partial_{0}^{q}\partial_{I}^{(k-n)}\Upsilon^{-2})\Upsilon^{4+2m_{1}+2m_{2}}\frac{\mathcal{U}^{(n)}}{\tilde{\mathcal{F}}^{2+2+2m_{1}+2m_{2}}}W^{1-p_{1}}\bar{W}^{1-p_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\partial_{0}^{q}\partial_{I}^{(k-n)}\Upsilon^{-2})\Upsilon^{4+2m}\frac{\mathcal{U}^{(n)}}{\tilde{\mathcal{F}}^{4+2m}}W^{1-p_{1}}\bar{W}^{1-p_{2}},

where
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}\equiv 2K_{I\bar{\jmath}}\left(-\partial_{\mu}z^{I}\partial^{\mu}\bar{z}^{\bar{\jmath}}+F^{I}\bar{F}^{\bar{\jmath}}\right)
;

\mathcal{U}^{(n)}
(
0\leq n\leq 4
) is the
n
-th derivative of the
function
\mathcal{U}(z^{I},\bar{z}^{\bar{\imath}})
with respect to
z^{I},\bar{z}^{I}
,
which are the lowest component of the matter chiral multiplets;
q=q_{1}+q_{2}
where
q_{1}

(
q_{2}
) is the order of the derivative with respect to the compensator scalar
s_{0}
(
s_{0}^{*}
);
p=p_{1}+p_{2}
where
p_{1}
(
p_{2}
) is the order of the derivative with respect to the field strength multiplet scalar
W
(
\bar{W}
);
m=m_{1}+m_{2}
where
m_{1}
(
m_{2}
) is the
order of the derivative with respect to the chiral projection multiplet scalar
T(\bar{w}^{2})
(
\bar{T}(w^{2})
);

is the order of the derivative with respect to the matter multiplet scalar
z^{I}
;

n
is the order of the derivative acting on the new term
\mathcal{U}
with respect to the matter multiplet;
q
is the total order of
derivative with respect to the compensator scalars
s_{0}
and
s_{0}^{*}
. An explicit form of the derivatives of

is given in
Appendix A.

The mass dimension
5
5

5

the mass dimensions of the multiplets’ lowest components are
[s_{0}]=1,[z^{I}]=0,[W]=3,[T]=0
, which gives
[\tilde{\mathcal{F}}]=2
.
of the derivatives of the
N
is
[N^{(r=q+p+m+k)}_{q,p,m,k}]=-3p-4m-2
. This implies that the mass dimension of the operator coupled to
N^{(r=q+p+m+k)}_{q,p,m,k}
must be equal to
3p+4m+2
.

Now, let us focus on the case such that
q=0
and
k=n
which gives the most singular fermionic terms in the limit
that the D-term vanishes. The most singular terms are those that contain the highest power of the auxiliary field
D
in the
denominator and therefore are the nonrenormalizable operators associated with the smallest UV cutoff mass scale. That is, we consider that the matter scalar derivatives act only on the new term
\mathcal{U}
and there are no the derivatives with respect to the compensator scalar. Then, we have

\displaystyle N^{(r=p+m+n)}_{p,m,n}=\Upsilon^{2+2m}\frac{\mathcal{U}^{(n)}}{\tilde{\mathcal{F}}^{4+2m}}W^{1-p_{1}}\bar{W}^{1-p_{2}}.

(7.146)

In particular, since
r=p+m+n
(
0\leq r\leq 4
), it reduces to

\displaystyle N_{i\ldots l}^{(r)}=N^{(r)}_{p,m,n}=\Upsilon^{2(1+r-n-p)}\frac{\mathcal{U}^{(n)}}{\tilde{\mathcal{F}}^{2(2+r-n-p)}}W^{1-p_{1}}\bar{W}^{1-p_{2}}.

(7.147)

Remember that we called
r
the total order of the derivatives acting on the
N
. Here
n
is the number of
derivatives acting on the
matter scalars in the new term
\mathcal{U}
; they produce
\mathcal{U}^{(n)}
. Finally,
p=p_{1}+p_{2}
is the sum of the number of derivatives w.r.t the multiplets
(W,\bar{W})
acting on
\mathcal{U}
.

Structure of the fermionic components

Next, let us explore the detailed structure of the chiral fermions of the superconformal multiplets. First of all, the compensator and matter fermions are given by

\displaystyle\chi^{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{L}\chi^{0},\qquad\chi^{I}=P_{L}\chi^{I}.

(7.148)

Note that
\chi^{0}
and
\chi^{I}
are fundamental fermions, and later in the
S
-gauge, the compensator chiral fermions will be replaced by the matter ones according to:
P_{L}\chi^{0}=\frac{1}{3}s_{0}K_{I}P_{L}\chi^{I}
and
P_{R}\chi^{0}=\frac{1}{3}s_{0}^{*}K_{\bar{I}}P_{R}\chi^{\bar{I}}
.

On the contrary, the other fermions
\chi^{W}
and
\chi^{T}
are composite. Hence, we need to find their specific structure.

First, the
\mathcal{W}(K)^{2}
-multiplet fermion, say
\chi^{W}
, is found to be

\displaystyle\chi^{W}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{L}\chi^{W}=\sqrt{2}P_{L}(-\gamma\cdot\hat{F}_{K}+2i\mathcal{D}_{K})\Lambda_{K}

(7.149)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\sqrt{2}P_{L}\gamma^{\mu\nu}\partial_{[\mu}\mathcal{B}_{\nu]}^{K}\Lambda_{K}-\sqrt{2}P_{L}\gamma^{\mu\nu}\bar{\psi}_{[\mu}\gamma_{\nu]}\Lambda_{K}\Lambda_{K}+2\sqrt{2}iP_{L}\mathcal{D}_{K}\Lambda_{K}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\sqrt{2}P_{L}\gamma^{\mu\nu}\partial_{[\mu}(iK_{I}\mathcal{D}_{\nu]}z^{I}-iK_{\bar{I}}\mathcal{D}_{\nu]}\bar{z}^{\bar{I}}+iK_{I\bar{J}}\bar{\chi}^{I}\gamma_{\nu]}\chi^{\bar{J}})\Lambda_{K}+2\sqrt{2}iP_{L}\mathcal{D}_{K}\Lambda_{K}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2i\gamma^{\mu\nu}\partial_{[\mu}(K_{I}\bar{\psi}_{\nu]}\chi^{I}-K_{\bar{I}}\bar{\chi}^{\bar{I}}\psi_{\nu]}-\sqrt{2}K_{I\bar{J}}\bar{\chi}^{I}\gamma_{\nu]}\chi^{\bar{J}})P_{L}\Lambda_{K}

\displaystyle+2\sqrt{2}i\bigg[2K_{I\bar{J}}\bigg(-\mathcal{D}_{\mu}z^{I}\mathcal{D}^{\mu}\bar{z}^{\bar{J}}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\chi}^{I}P_{L}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\chi^{\bar{J}}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\chi}^{\bar{J}}P_{R}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\chi^{I}+F^{I}\bar{F}^{\bar{J}}\bigg)

\displaystyle+K_{IJ\bar{K}}\bigg(-\bar{\chi}^{I}\chi^{J}\bar{F}^{\bar{K}}+\bar{\chi}^{I}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}z^{J})\chi^{\bar{K}}\bigg)+K_{\bar{I}\bar{J}K}\bigg(-\bar{\chi}^{\bar{I}}\chi^{\bar{J}}F^{K}+\bar{\chi}^{\bar{I}}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{z}^{\bar{J}})\chi^{K}\bigg)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}K_{IJ\bar{K}\bar{L}}(\bar{\chi}^{I}P_{L}\chi^{J})(\bar{\chi}^{\bar{K}}P_{R}\chi^{\bar{L}})\bigg]P_{L}\Lambda_{K}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{2}i\tilde{\mathcal{F}}(P_{L}\Lambda_{K})_{1f}+\cdots+7~\textrm{fermions}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{2}i\tilde{\mathcal{F}}(-\sqrt{2}iK_{\bar{I}J}[(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}z^{J})_{0f}\chi^{\bar{I}}-\bar{F}^{\bar{I}}\chi^{J}])+\cdots+7~\textrm{fermions}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\tilde{\mathcal{F}}K_{\bar{I}J}[(\cancel{\partial}z^{J})\chi^{\bar{I}}-\bar{F}^{\bar{I}}\chi^{J}]+\cdots+7~\textrm{fermions},

where
P_{L}\Lambda_{K}=-\sqrt{2}iK_{\bar{I}J}[(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}z^{J})\chi^{\bar{I}}-\bar{F}^{\bar{I}}\chi^{J}]-\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}K_{\bar{I}\bar{J}K}\chi^{K}\bar{\chi}^{\bar{I}}\chi^{J}
. Note that the composite fermion
\chi^{W}
contains powers of the
matter fermions ranging from one to seven, and it is nonvanishing on-shell.

Second, the chiral projection multiplet
T(\bar{w}^{2})
fermions are found to be as follows:

\displaystyle\chi^{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{L}\chi^{T}=-\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}P_{L}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\mathcal{Z}_{\bar{w}}+\Lambda_{\bar{w}})=-\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg[\frac{(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\mathcal{Z}_{\bar{W}})}{\Upsilon^{2}}-\frac{(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\Upsilon)\mathcal{Z}_{\bar{W}}}{\Upsilon^{3}}-2\frac{\mathcal{C}_{\bar{W}}}{\Upsilon^{3}}P_{L}\Lambda_{\Upsilon}-\frac{i\cancel{\mathcal{B}}_{\Upsilon}\mathcal{Z}_{\bar{W}}}{\Upsilon^{3}}\bigg]_{\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon}=0}

(7.150)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Upsilon^{2}}\bigg[~\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\chi^{\bar{W}}-\frac{(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\Upsilon)\chi^{\bar{W}}}{\Upsilon}-2\frac{\bar{\Lambda}_{K}P_{R}\Lambda_{K}}{\Upsilon}P_{L}\Lambda_{\Upsilon}-\frac{i\cancel{\mathcal{B}}_{\Upsilon}\chi^{\bar{W}}}{\Upsilon}~\bigg]_{\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon}=0}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Upsilon^{2}}\bigg[~(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\chi^{\bar{W}})_{1f}-\frac{[(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\Upsilon)_{0f}+i(\cancel{\mathcal{B}}_{\Upsilon})_{0f}]}{\Upsilon}(\chi^{\bar{W}})_{1f}~\bigg]+\cdots+9~\textrm{fermions},

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Upsilon^{2}}\bigg[~4(\cancel{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{F}})K_{\bar{I}J}[(\cancel{\partial}z^{J})\chi^{\bar{I}}-\bar{F}^{\bar{I}}\chi^{J}]

\displaystyle-\Big(\frac{2}{s_{0}^{*}}\cancel{\partial}s_{0}^{*}-\frac{2}{3}K_{\bar{K}}\cancel{\partial}\bar{z}^{\bar{K}}-2\gamma^{\mu}(b_{\mu}+iA_{\mu})\Big)4\tilde{\mathcal{F}}K_{\bar{I}J}[(\cancel{\partial}z^{J})\chi^{\bar{I}}-\bar{F}^{\bar{I}}\chi^{J}]~\bigg]_{1f}+\cdots+9~\textrm{fermions},

\displaystyle\frac{4}{\Upsilon^{2}}(\cancel{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{F}})K_{\bar{I}J}[(\cancel{\partial}z^{J})\chi^{\bar{I}}-\bar{F}^{\bar{I}}\chi^{J}]+(\chi^{T})_{1f}|_{\tilde{\mathcal{F}}^{1}}+\cdots+9~\textrm{fermions},

Note that

\displaystyle-3\sqrt{2}WP_{L}\phi_{\mu}.

to find

\displaystyle-4\tilde{\mathcal{F}}K_{\bar{I}J}(\cancel{\partial}\bar{F}^{\bar{I}})\chi^{J}.

Here
\approx
means equality up to terms proportional to the equations of motion of free massless matter fields. Such
terms produce only terms that contain additional factors of the matter field masses in the numerator and therefore give rise to either renormalizable operators or nonrenormalizable operators weighted by a mass scale higher than that associated to those
terms that do not vanish on shell. Some details of the above calculation are as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\mu}z^{I}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{\mu}z^{I}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\bar{\psi}_{\mu}\chi^{I},

(7.156)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\bar{W}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\bar{W}=\bar{\Lambda}_{K}P_{R}\Lambda_{K}=2~\textrm{fermions}+4~\textrm{fermions}+6~\textrm{fermions},

(7.157)

\displaystyle P_{L}\Lambda_{K}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{2}iK_{\bar{I}J}[(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}z^{J})\chi^{\bar{I}}-\bar{F}^{\bar{I}}\chi^{J}]-\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}K_{\bar{I}\bar{J}K}\chi^{K}\bar{\chi}^{\bar{I}}\chi^{J},

(7.158)

\displaystyle(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\chi^{\bar{W}})_{1f}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{2}i(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\tilde{\mathcal{F}})_{0f}(P_{L}\Lambda_{K})_{1f}+2\sqrt{2}i\tilde{\mathcal{F}}(P_{L}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\Lambda_{K})_{1f},

(7.159)

\displaystyle\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\Lambda_{K}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\gamma\cdot\mathcal{D}\Lambda_{K},

(7.160)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\mu}\Lambda_{K}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\partial_{\mu}-\frac{3}{2}b_{\mu}+\frac{1}{4}\omega_{\mu}^{ab}\gamma_{ab}-\frac{3}{2}i\gamma_{*}\mathcal{A}_{\mu}\Big)\Lambda_{K}-\Big(\frac{1}{4}\gamma\cdot\hat{F}^{K}+\frac{1}{2}i\gamma_{*}\mathcal{D}_{K}\Big)\psi_{\mu},

(7.161)

\displaystyle(P_{L}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\Lambda_{K})_{1f}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\gamma^{\mu}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}|_{\psi=0}(P_{R}\Lambda_{K})_{1f}+\frac{i}{2}\tilde{\mathcal{F}}\gamma^{\mu}\psi_{\mu},

(7.162)

\displaystyle(\gamma\cdot\hat{F}^{K})_{0f}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0,\qquad\mathcal{D}_{K}|_{0f}=\tilde{\mathcal{F}}

(7.163)

\displaystyle(\hat{F}^{K}_{\mu\nu})_{0f}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2\partial_{[\mu}\mathcal{B}_{\nu]}^{K})_{0f}=2i\partial_{[\mu}(K_{I}\partial_{\nu]}z^{I}-K_{\bar{I}}\partial_{\nu]}\bar{z}^{\bar{I}})=2i(\partial_{[\mu}K_{I}\partial_{\nu]}z^{I}-\partial_{[\mu}K_{\bar{I}}\partial_{\nu]}\bar{z}^{\bar{I}})

(7.164)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2i(K_{IJ}\partial_{[\mu}z^{(J}\partial_{\nu]}z^{I)}-K_{\bar{I}\bar{J}}\partial_{[\mu}z^{(\bar{J}}\partial_{\nu]}\bar{z}^{\bar{I})})=0,

\displaystyle(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\Upsilon)_{0f}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\cancel{\partial}-2\gamma^{\mu}b_{\mu}-2i\gamma^{\mu}A_{\mu})\Upsilon

(7.165)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\cancel{\partial}s_{0})s_{0}^{*}e^{-K/3}+s_{0}(\cancel{\partial}s_{0}^{*})e^{-K/3}+s_{0}s_{0}^{*}e^{-K/3}(-\frac{1}{3}\cancel{\partial}K)-2\gamma^{\mu}(b_{\mu}+iA_{\mu})\Upsilon

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Upsilon(\frac{1}{s_{0}}\cancel{\partial}s_{0}+\frac{1}{s_{0}^{*}}\cancel{\partial}s_{0}^{*}-\frac{1}{3}K_{I}\cancel{\partial}z^{I}-\frac{1}{3}K_{\bar{I}}\cancel{\partial}\bar{z}^{\bar{I}}-2\gamma^{\mu}(b_{\mu}+iA_{\mu})),

\displaystyle i(\cancel{\mathcal{B}}_{\Upsilon})_{0f}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Upsilon(-\frac{1}{s_{0}}\cancel{\partial}s_{0}+\frac{1}{s_{0}^{*}}\cancel{\partial}s_{0}^{*}+\frac{1}{3}K_{I}\cancel{\partial}z^{I}-\frac{1}{3}K_{\bar{I}}\cancel{\partial}\bar{z}^{\bar{I}}),

\displaystyle\frac{(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\Upsilon)_{0f}+i(\cancel{\mathcal{B}}_{\Upsilon})_{0f}}{\Upsilon}

(7.166)

Finally, we present here the chiral fermions of the superconformal multiplets up to multiple fermion terms.

\displaystyle\chi^{i}=\begin{cases}\chi^{0},\\
\chi^{I},\\
\chi^{W}=4\tilde{\mathcal{F}}K_{\bar{I}J}[(\cancel{\partial}z^{J})\chi^{\bar{I}}-\bar{F}^{\bar{I}}\chi^{J}]+\cdots 7~\textrm{fermions},\\
\chi^{T}=\frac{1}{\Upsilon^{2}}\bigg[~4(\cancel{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{F}})K_{\bar{I}J}[(\cancel{\partial}z^{J})\chi^{\bar{I}}-\bar{F}^{\bar{I}}\chi^{J}]-\Big(\frac{2}{s_{0}^{*}}\cancel{\partial}s_{0}^{*}-\frac{2}{3}K_{\bar{K}}\cancel{\partial}\bar{z}^{\bar{K}}-2\gamma^{\mu}(b_{\mu}+iA_{\mu})\Big)\\
\qquad\qquad\quad\times 4\tilde{\mathcal{F}}K_{\bar{I}J}[(\cancel{\partial}z^{J})\chi^{\bar{I}}-\bar{F}^{\bar{I}}\chi^{J}]~\bigg]_{1f}+\cdots+9~\textrm{fermions},\end{cases}

where

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{F}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2K_{I\bar{J}}\left(-\partial_{\mu}z^{I}\partial^{\mu}\bar{z}^{\bar{J}}+F^{I}\bar{F}^{\bar{J}}\right)

\displaystyle\cancel{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{F}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2(\cancel{\partial}K_{I\bar{J}})\left(-\partial_{\mu}z^{I}\partial^{\mu}\bar{z}^{\bar{J}}+F^{I}\bar{F}^{\bar{J}}\right)

\displaystyle+2K_{I\bar{J}}\left(-(\cancel{\partial}\partial_{\mu}z^{I})\partial^{\mu}\bar{z}^{\bar{J}}-\partial_{\mu}z^{I}(\cancel{\partial}\partial^{\mu}\bar{z}^{\bar{J}})+(\cancel{\partial}F^{I})\bar{F}^{\bar{J}}+F^{I}(\cancel{\partial}\bar{F}^{\bar{J}})\right).

Notice that none of the chiral fermions
\chi^{i}
vanish on-shell, and that only
\chi^{T}
dependens on
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}
and includes the factor of
\Upsilon^{-2}
. All of these properties affect the mass dimension of the expansion coefficients of the nonrenormalizable Lagrangians.

We finally expand
W
and
\bar{W}
as follows:

\displaystyle W

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2K_{\bar{\imath}J}[\bar{\chi}^{J}(\overline{\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{z}^{\bar{\imath}}})-\bar{F}^{\bar{\imath}}\bar{\chi}^{J}]K_{{\bar{\imath}}^{\prime}J^{\prime}}[(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{z}^{{\bar{\imath}}^{\prime}})\chi^{J^{\prime}}-F^{J^{\prime}}\chi^{{\bar{\imath}}^{\prime}}]

(7.167)

\displaystyle-K_{{\bar{\imath}}J}[\bar{\chi}^{J}(\overline{\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{z}^{\bar{\imath}}})-\bar{F}^{\bar{\imath}}\bar{\chi}^{J}]K_{\bar{\imath}^{\prime}\bar{\jmath}^{\prime}K^{\prime}}[\chi^{K^{\prime}}\bar{\chi}^{\bar{\imath}^{\prime}}\chi^{\bar{\jmath}^{\prime}}]

\displaystyle-K_{\bar{\imath}\bar{\jmath}K}[\bar{\chi}^{\bar{\jmath}}\chi^{\bar{\imath}}\bar{\chi}^{K}]K_{{\bar{\imath}}^{\prime}J^{\prime}}[(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{z}^{{\bar{\imath}}^{\prime}})\chi^{J^{\prime}}-F^{J^{\prime}}\chi^{{\bar{\imath}}^{\prime}}]

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}K_{\bar{\imath}\bar{\jmath}K}[\bar{\chi}^{\bar{\jmath}}\chi^{\bar{\imath}}\bar{\chi}^{K}]K_{\bar{\imath}^{\prime}\bar{\jmath}^{\prime}K^{\prime}}[\chi^{K^{\prime}}\bar{\chi}^{\bar{\imath}^{\prime}}\chi^{\bar{\jmath}^{\prime}}],

\displaystyle\bar{W}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(W)^{*}.

(7.168)

Notice that
W
and
\bar{W}
can be represented by products of two, four, and six fundamental fermions.

7.3.3
Additional gauge fixing for physical theory in the liberated supergravity

First of all, we consider the conventional superconformal gauge which is chosen by

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\Upsilon}=\Upsilon=1\Longleftrightarrow s_{0}\bar{s}_{0}e^{-K/3}=1,

(7.169)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon}=0\implies P_{L}\chi^{0}-\frac{1}{3}s_{0}K_{I}P_{L}\chi^{I}=0\quad\&\quad P_{R}\chi^{0}-\frac{1}{3}s_{0}^{*}K_{\bar{I}}P_{R}\chi^{\bar{I}}=0,

(7.170)

\displaystyle s_{0}=\bar{s}_{0}\implies s_{0}=\bar{s}_{0}=e^{K/6},

(7.171)

\displaystyle b_{\mu}=0.

(7.172)

Note that the first condition is the
D
-gauge fixing which gets us to the Einstein frame; the second one is the improved
S
-gauge fixing; the third one is the
A
-gauge fixing; the last one is the
K
-gauge fixing.

To compare our results with the formulation of liberated supergravity in
[
71
]
, we choose a gauge given by

\displaystyle\Lambda_{K}=0\Longleftrightarrow\chi^{W}=\chi^{T}=0.

(7.173)

In both the conventional superconformal gauge (
7.170
,
7.171
,
7.172
) and in (
7.173
), the relevant multiplets are

\displaystyle S_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(e^{K/6},\frac{1}{3}e^{K/6}K_{I}P_{L}\chi^{I},F^{0}\right),\qquad\bar{S}_{0}=\left(e^{K/6},\frac{1}{3}e^{K/6}K_{\bar{I}}P_{R}\chi^{\bar{I}},F^{0*}\right),

(7.174)

\displaystyle z^{I}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(z^{I},P_{L}\chi^{I},F^{I}\right),\qquad\bar{z}^{\bar{I}}=\left(\bar{z}^{\bar{I}},P_{R}\chi^{\bar{I}},\bar{F}^{\bar{I}}\right),

(7.175)

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}^{2}(K)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(W,P_{L}\chi^{W},F^{W}\right)=\left(0,0,\hat{F}_{K}^{-}\cdot\hat{F}_{K}^{-}-\mathcal{D}^{2}_{K}\right),

(7.176)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{W}}^{2}(K)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\bar{W},P_{R}\chi^{\bar{W}},\bar{F}^{\bar{W}}\right)=\left(0,0,\hat{F}_{K}^{+}\cdot\hat{F}_{K}^{+}-(\mathcal{D}^{*}_{K})^{2}\right),

(7.177)

\displaystyle T(\bar{w}^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(T,P_{L}\chi^{T},F^{T}\right)=\left(-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{K}_{\bar{w}},0,\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{D}_{\bar{w}}\right),

(7.178)

\displaystyle\bar{T}(w^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\bar{X}^{\bar{T}},P_{R}\chi^{\bar{T}},\bar{F}^{\bar{T}}\right)=\left(-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{K}_{\bar{w}}^{*},0,\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{D}_{\bar{w}}^{*}\right).

(7.179)

Let us further observe that all the terms coupled to
N
and its derivatives with respect to
0,I,T
vanish because
N
contains the product of
W\bar{W}
, which is zero in the liberated gauge, i.e.
W=\bar{W}=0
. We see that all the fermionic terms coupled to the derivatives of
N
with respect to
W
or
T
vanish as well, because these terms always couple to the fermions
\chi^{W}
and
\chi^{T}
which vanish in the gauge. The only non-vanishing contribution is given by only the bosonic term,
N_{W\bar{W}}F^{W}\bar{F}^{\bar{W}}
which gives us the new term
\mathcal{U}
to the scalar potential. Therefore, in both gauges, the liberated supergravity Lagrangian is specified by

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{Lib}=\mathcal{L}_{SUGRA}+\mathcal{L}_{NEW},

(7.180)

where
\mathcal{L}_{SUGRA}
is the standard supergravity Lagrangian which contains the auxiliary fields
F^{0},F^{I}
and
\mathcal{L}_{NEW}=\mathcal{U}(z^{I},\bar{z}^{\bar{I}})
. Then, with this Lagrangian, after solving the equations of motion for the auxiliary fields, we can obtain the usual supergravity action in addition to the new term.

7.4
Spectroscophy for Non-renormalizable Interactions in the Liberated
\mathcal{N}=1
Supergravity

In this section, we investigate the suppression of the nonrenormalizable fermionic terms in the liberated
\mathcal{N}=1
supergravity in the superconformal formalism. To do this, we need to recall the EFT expansion reviewed in Sec.
3.4
, and consider the structure of EFT expansion in Eq. (
3.18
) and constraint given in Eq. (
3.19
). Hence, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{EFT}\supset\sum_{\delta\geq 0}^{\textrm{Finite N}}\frac{C^{\delta}}{M^{\delta-d}}\mathcal{O}^{(\delta)}\sim\sum_{\delta\geq 0}^{\textrm{Finite N}}\frac{1}{\Lambda_{cut}^{\delta-d}}\mathcal{O}^{(\delta)}\implies\frac{C^{\delta}}{M^{\delta-d}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\Lambda_{cut}^{\delta-d}}

where
\Lambda_{cut}
is a cutoff scale;
M
is a characteristic mass scale of a theory;
C^{\delta}
is a dimensionless Wilson coefficient, and
\mathcal{O}^{(\delta)}
is an effective field operator with the mass dimension
\delta
. In our model, the dimensionless Wilson coefficient will be dependent on the liberated term
\mathcal{U}
, and the mass scale
M
will depend on
\mathcal{F}
. Hence, the key result we will find in this section is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}^{(n)}\lesssim\begin{cases}\mathcal{F}^{2(4-n)}\left(\dfrac{M_{pl}}{\Lambda_{cut}}\right)^{2(4-n)}\quad\textrm{where}\quad 0\leq n\leq 2\quad\textrm{for}~N_{mat}=1,\\
\mathcal{F}^{2(6-n)}\left(\dfrac{M_{pl}}{\Lambda_{cut}}\right)^{2(6-n)}\quad\textrm{where}\quad 0\leq n\leq 4\quad\textrm{for}~N_{mat}\geq 2,\end{cases}

(7.181)

where
n
is the total number of derivatives with respect to the chiral matter multiplets
z^{I}
;
\mathcal{F}\equiv\left<K_{I\bar{J}}F^{I}\bar{F}^{\bar{J}}\right>
is the dimensionless vacuum expectation value of
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}
;
\mathcal{U}(z,\bar{z})
is called “liberated term” defined as a dimensionless, gauge-invariant, general real functon of the matter scalars
z^{I}
’s, and
\mathcal{U}^{(n)}
is the
n
-th derivative of the liberated term.

7.4.1
Fermionic terms in liberated
\mathcal{N}=1
supergravity

The component Lagrangians of the liberated supergravity is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{NEW}\equiv[\mathbb{N}]_{D}e^{-1}

\displaystyle=N_{i\bar{\jmath}}\bigg(-\mathcal{D}_{\mu}z^{i}\mathcal{D}^{\mu}\bar{z}^{\bar{\jmath}}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\chi}^{i}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\chi^{\bar{\jmath}}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\chi}^{\bar{\jmath}}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\chi^{i}+F^{i}\bar{F}^{\bar{\jmath}}\bigg)+\frac{1}{2}\bigg[N_{ij\bar{k}}\Big(-\bar{\chi}^{i}\chi^{j}\bar{F}^{\bar{k}}+\bar{\chi}^{i}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}z^{j})\chi^{\bar{k}}\Big)+h.c.\bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}N_{ij\bar{k}\bar{l}}\bar{\chi}^{i}\chi^{j}\bar{\chi}^{\bar{k}}\chi^{\bar{l}}

\displaystyle+\bigg[\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}}\bar{\psi}\cdot\gamma\bigg(N_{i\bar{\jmath}}F^{i}\chi^{\bar{\jmath}}-N_{i\bar{\jmath}}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{z}^{\bar{\jmath}}\chi^{i}-\frac{1}{2}N_{ij\bar{k}}\chi^{\bar{k}}\bar{\chi}^{i}\chi^{j}\bigg)+\frac{1}{8}i\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\bar{\psi}_{\mu}\gamma_{\nu}\psi_{\rho}\bigg(N_{i}\mathcal{D}_{\sigma}z^{i}+\frac{1}{2}N_{i\bar{\jmath}}\bar{\chi}^{i}\gamma_{\sigma}\chi^{\bar{\jmath}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}N_{i}\bar{\psi}_{\sigma}\chi^{i}\bigg)+h.c.\bigg]+\frac{1}{6}N\left(-R(\omega)+\frac{1}{2}\bar{\psi}_{\mu}\gamma^{\mu\nu\rho}R^{\prime}_{\nu\rho}(Q)\right)-\frac{1}{6\sqrt{2}}\left(N_{i}\bar{\chi}^{i}+N_{\bar{\imath}}\bar{\chi}^{\bar{\imath}}\right)\gamma^{\mu\nu}R^{\prime}_{\mu\nu}(Q),

(7.182)

First of all, focusing only on matter couplings, i.e. looking at terms independent of
\psi
, we read the following terms from Eq. (
7.182
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{F1}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle-N_{i\bar{\jmath}}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}z^{i}\mathcal{D}^{\mu}\bar{z}^{\bar{\jmath}}\Big|_{\psi=0},\quad\mathcal{L}_{F2}\equiv-\frac{1}{2}N_{i\bar{\jmath}}\bar{\chi}^{i}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\chi^{\bar{\jmath}}\Big|_{\psi=0},

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{F3}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle-N_{i\bar{\jmath}}F^{i}\bar{F}^{\bar{\jmath}}\Big|_{\psi=0},\quad\mathcal{L}_{F4}\equiv-\frac{1}{2}N_{ij\bar{k}}\bar{\chi}^{i}\chi^{j}\bar{F}^{\bar{k}}\Big|_{\psi=0},

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{F5}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}N_{ij\bar{k}}\bar{\chi}^{i}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}z^{j})\chi^{\bar{k}}\Big|_{\psi=0},

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{F6}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}N_{ij\bar{k}\bar{l}}\bar{\chi}^{i}\chi^{j}\bar{\chi}^{\bar{k}}\chi^{\bar{l}}\Big|_{\psi=0},\quad\mathcal{L}_{F7}\equiv-\frac{N}{6}R(\omega)|_{\psi=0}.

(7.183)

Here, we observe that the fermionic terms in the effective Lagrangian contain couplings to the function
\mathcal{U}

and its derivatives since
N\propto\mathcal{U}
.

The general structure of the fermionic terms can be found as a power series in derivatives of the composite multiplet
N
(i.e.
N_{i},N_{i\bar{\jmath}},N_{ij\bar{k}}
and
N_{ij\bar{k}\bar{l}}
). The
r
-th derivative of
N
, denoted with

N_{i\ldots\bar{l}}^{(r)}
has the following generic form

\displaystyle N_{i\ldots\bar{l}}^{(r)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle N^{(r=q+p+m+k)}_{q,p,m,k}=(\partial_{0}^{q}\partial_{I}^{(k-n)}\Upsilon^{-2})\Upsilon^{4+2m}\frac{\mathcal{U}^{(n)}}{\tilde{\mathcal{F}}^{4+2m}}W^{1-p_{1}}\bar{W}^{1-p_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big((-1)^{q_{1}+q_{2}}(q_{1}+1)!(q_{2}+1)!s_{0}^{-(2+q_{1})}{s_{0}^{*}}^{-(2+q_{2})}(\partial_{I}^{(k-n)}e^{2K/3})\Big)\Upsilon^{4+2m}\frac{\mathcal{U}^{(n)}}{\tilde{\mathcal{F}}^{4+2m}}W^{1-p_{1}}\bar{W}^{1-p_{2}}.

where

\displaystyle W

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2K_{\bar{\imath}J}[\bar{\chi}^{J}(\overline{\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{z}^{\bar{\imath}}})-\bar{F}^{\bar{\imath}}\bar{\chi}^{J}]K_{{\bar{\imath}}^{\prime}J^{\prime}}[(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{z}^{{\bar{\imath}}^{\prime}})\chi^{J^{\prime}}-F^{J^{\prime}}\chi^{{\bar{\imath}}^{\prime}}]-K_{{\bar{\imath}}J}[\bar{\chi}^{J}(\overline{\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{z}^{\bar{\imath}}})-\bar{F}^{\bar{\imath}}\bar{\chi}^{J}]K_{\bar{\imath}^{\prime}\bar{\jmath}^{\prime}K^{\prime}}[\chi^{K^{\prime}}\bar{\chi}^{\bar{\imath}^{\prime}}\chi^{\bar{\jmath}^{\prime}}]

(7.185)

\displaystyle-K_{\bar{\imath}\bar{\jmath}K}[\bar{\chi}^{\bar{\jmath}}\chi^{\bar{\imath}}\bar{\chi}^{K}]K_{{\bar{\imath}}^{\prime}J^{\prime}}[(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{z}^{{\bar{\imath}}^{\prime}})\chi^{J^{\prime}}-F^{J^{\prime}}\chi^{{\bar{\imath}}^{\prime}}]-\frac{1}{2}K_{\bar{\imath}\bar{\jmath}K}[\bar{\chi}^{\bar{\jmath}}\chi^{\bar{\imath}}\bar{\chi}^{K}]K_{\bar{\imath}^{\prime}\bar{\jmath}^{\prime}K^{\prime}}[\chi^{K^{\prime}}\bar{\chi}^{\bar{\imath}^{\prime}}\chi^{\bar{\jmath}^{\prime}}],

\displaystyle\bar{W}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(W)^{*},

(7.186)

\tilde{\mathcal{F}}\equiv 2K_{I\bar{\jmath}}\left(-\partial_{\mu}z^{I}\partial^{\mu}\bar{z}^{\bar{\jmath}}+F^{I}\bar{F}^{\bar{\jmath}}\right)
;

\mathcal{U}^{(n)}
(
0\leq n\leq 4
) is the
n
-th derivative of the
function
\mathcal{U}(z^{I},\bar{z}^{\bar{\imath}})
with respect to
z^{I},\bar{z}^{I}
,
which are the lowest component of the matter chiral multiplets;
q=q_{1}+q_{2}
where
q_{1}

(
q_{2}
) is the order of the derivative w.r.t. the compensator scalar
s_{0}
(
s_{0}^{*}
);
p=p_{1}+p_{2}
where
p_{1}
(
p_{2}
) is the order of the derivative w.r.t. the field strength multiplet scalar
W
(
\bar{W}
);
m=m_{1}+m_{2}
where
m_{1}
(
m_{2}
) is the
order of the derivative w.r.t. the chiral projection multiplet scalar
T(\bar{w}^{2})
(
\bar{T}(w^{2})
);
k
is the order of the derivative w.r.t. the matter multiplet scalar
z^{I}
;

n
is the order of the derivative acting on the new term
\mathcal{U}
w.r.t. the matter multiplet;
q
is the total order of
derivative w.r.t. the compensator scalars
s_{0}
and
s_{0}^{*}
.

To find restrictions on
V_{NEW}
coming from fermionic terms, we have to identify the most singular terms in the
Lagrangian. These terms can be found using the fact that powers of
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}
in the denominator may lead to a
singularity which gets stronger when
m
increases by taking more derivatives with respect to the lowest component of the
multiplet
T(\bar{w}^{2})
as seen from Eq. (
). Hence, we will investigate the fermionic terms containing only derivatives with respect to the chiral projection
and matter scalar indices, i.e.
T
and
I
, in order to find the terms coupled to
\mathcal{U}^{(n)}
that contain the
maximal inverse powers of
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}
. They are those with
q=p=0
and
k=n
. We note in particular that
if our theory has a single chiral matter multiplet then the most singular terms are
found to be the couplings to the derivatives proportional to
N_{T\bar{T}}
,
N_{WT\bar{T}}
,
N_{W\bar{W}T\bar{T}}

while for two or more chiral matter multiplets they are
N_{TT\bar{T}\bar{T}}
. The latter terms vanish identically for a
single multiplet because of Fermi statistics.

First of all, let us examine the single matter chiral multiplet case. Due to Fermi statistics, the possible fermionic terms are
proportional only to three tems,
\mathcal{U}^{(0)}
,
\mathcal{U}^{(1)}
, and
\mathcal{U}^{(2)}
, so that the maximal
order of the derivative with respect to the chiral projection that can appear in the Lagrangians scalar is two and
appears in the terms
proportional to
N_{T\bar{T}}
,
N_{WT\bar{T}}
, and
N_{W\bar{W}T\bar{T}}
. To show that such terms do not
vanish consider

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{F2}|_{q=p=0,k=n}

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{2+2m}\frac{\mathcal{U}^{(n)}}{\tilde{\mathcal{F}}^{4+2m}}W\bar{W}\bigg[-\frac{1}{2}\Big((\bar{\chi}^{I})^{n_{1}}(\Upsilon^{-2}4(\cancel{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{F}})K_{\bar{\imath}J}(\cancel{\partial}z^{J})\bar{\chi}^{\bar{\imath}}P_{R})^{m_{1}}\Big)

(7.187)

\displaystyle\times\Big((\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\chi^{I})^{n_{2}}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}(\Upsilon^{-2}4(\cancel{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{F}})K_{\bar{\imath}J}(\cancel{\partial}z^{J})P_{R}\chi^{\bar{\imath}}))^{m_{2}}\Big)\bigg]_{\psi=0},

where
m=m_{1}+m_{2}
,
n=n_{1}+n_{2}
, and
2=m+n
. Restoring the mass dimensions by fixing the super-Weyl gauge
6
6

6

Here, we use the convention of the superconformal formalism that all physical bosonic and fermionic matter
fields have dimensions 0 and 1/2 respectively and
\mathcal{F}
has dimension 2 while the compensator

s_{0}
has dimension 1
[
8
]
. Through dimensional analysis, we find
[\mathcal{D}_{\mu}]=1,[z^{i}]\equiv 0+[i],[\chi^{i}]\equiv\frac{1}{2}+[i],[F^{i}]\equiv 1+[i]
where
i=0,I,W,T
and

[0]=1,[I]=0,[W]=3,[T]=0
.
(i.e.
\Upsilon=M_{pl}^{2}
,
s_{0}=s_{0}^{*}=M_{pl}e^{K/6}
,
P_{L}\chi^{0}=\frac{1}{3}s_{0}K_{I}P_{L}\chi^{I}=\frac{1}{3}M_{pl}e^{K/6}K_{I}P_{L}\chi^{I}
, and
b_{\mu}=0
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{F2}|_{q=p=0,k=n}

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle M_{pl}^{2(2+2m)}\frac{\mathcal{U}^{(n)}}{\tilde{\mathcal{F}}^{4+2m}}W\bar{W}\bigg[-\frac{1}{2}\Big((\bar{\chi}^{I})^{n_{1}}(M_{pl}^{-4}4(\cancel{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{F}})K_{\bar{\imath}J}(\cancel{\partial}z^{J})\bar{\chi}^{\bar{\imath}}P_{R})^{m_{1}}\Big)

(7.188)

\displaystyle\times\Big((\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\chi^{I})^{n_{2}}(M_{pl}^{-4}(4\cancel{\mathcal{D}}(\cancel{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{F}})K_{\bar{\imath}J}(\cancel{\partial}z^{J})P_{R}\chi^{\bar{\imath}}))^{m_{2}}\Big)\bigg]_{\psi=0}\approx M_{pl}^{4}\frac{\mathcal{U}^{(n)}}{\tilde{\mathcal{F}}^{4+2m}}\mathcal{O}_{F}^{(\delta)},\quad

where we require
m_{a}+n_{a}=1
for
a=1,2
since we are studying the second derivative term
N_{i\bar{\jmath}}
coupled to
\bar{\chi}^{i}
and
\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\chi^{\bar{\jmath}}
.
Redefining
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}
to be dimensionless by
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}\rightarrow M_{pl}^{2}\tilde{\mathcal{F}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{F2}|_{q=p=0,k=n}\supset M_{pl}^{-4-2m}\frac{\mathcal{U}^{(n)}}{\tilde{\mathcal{F}}^{4+2m}}\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\prime(\delta)}.

(7.189)

We then find
\delta=8+4m
by trivial dimensional analysis because the Lagrangian has mass dimension 4.
Then, since
2=m+n
, we find that the most singular term is

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{F2}|_{q=p=0,k=n}\supset M_{pl}^{2(n-4)}\frac{\mathcal{U}^{(n)}}{\tilde{\mathcal{F}}^{2(4-n)}}\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\prime(2(6-n))}.

(7.190)

Next we consider the general case with several multiplets. We shall focus on the fourth derivative term denoted by
N_{ij\bar{k}\bar{l}}
, which gives a four-fermion term. Also, we have to consider the four-fermion product made only of
the
chiral fermions with
i=0,I,T
because they do not contribute one
power of the F-term
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}
in the numerator, which would reduce the number of inverse power of the F-term

\tilde{\mathcal{F}}
. This is because the overall factor of
\chi^{W}
contains such linear dependence.
The effective fermionic Lagrangian (
7.183
) reads then as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{F6}|_{q=p=0,k=n}

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{2+2m}\frac{\mathcal{U}^{(n)}}{\tilde{\mathcal{F}}^{4+2m}}W\bar{W}\Big(\frac{1}{4}(\chi^{I})^{n}(4\Upsilon^{-2}(\cancel{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{F}})K_{\bar{\imath}J}(\cancel{\partial}z^{J})P_{R}\chi^{\bar{\imath}})^{m}\Big).

(7.191)

After the super-Weyl gauge fixing we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{F6}|_{q=p=0,k=n}

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle M_{pl}^{2(2+2m)}\frac{\mathcal{U}^{(n)}}{\tilde{\mathcal{F}}^{4+2m}}W\bar{W}\Big(\frac{1}{4}(\chi^{I})^{n}(4M_{pl}^{-4}(\cancel{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{F}})K_{\bar{\imath}J}(\cancel{\partial}z^{J})P_{R}\chi^{\bar{\imath}})^{m}\Big)

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle cM_{pl}^{4}\frac{\mathcal{U}^{(n)}}{\tilde{\mathcal{F}}^{4+2m}}\mathcal{O}_{F}^{(\delta)}.

where
\mathcal{O}(1)\lesssim c\lesssim\mathcal{O}(10^{3})
. After doing the same dimensional analysis as in the
single-multiplet case, we obtain
\delta=8+4m
and

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{F6}|_{q=p=0,k=n}\supset cM_{pl}^{-4-2m}\frac{\mathcal{U}^{(n)}}{\tilde{\mathcal{F}}^{4+2m}}\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\prime(8+2m)}.

(7.193)

Then, since
4=m+n
, we can write the most singular terms as

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{F6}|_{q=p=0,k=n}\supset cM_{pl}^{2(n-6)}\frac{\mathcal{U}^{(n)}}{\tilde{\mathcal{F}}^{2(6-n)}}\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\prime(2(8-n))},

(7.194)

Since we are going to use liberated supergravity to describe time-dependent backgrounds such as slow-roll inflation
we need to look more closely at the structure of
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}
. From its definition

\tilde{\mathcal{F}}\equiv 2K_{I\bar{\jmath}}\left(-\partial_{\mu}z^{I}\partial^{\mu}\bar{z}^{\bar{\jmath}}+F^{I}\bar{F}^{\bar{\jmath}}\right)
,
we find
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}\equiv 2K_{I\bar{\jmath}}\left(\dot{z}^{I}\dot{\bar{z}}^{\bar{\jmath}}+F^{I}\bar{F}^{\bar{\jmath}}\right)>0

whenever spatial gradients can be neglected. We see that the most singular behaviors of the fermionic terms arises
when
\dot{z}^{I}=0
. By expanding
\tilde{F}
around a static vacuum and
reserving the notation
\mathcal{F}
for the expectation value
\left<K_{I\bar{\jmath}}F^{I}\bar{F}^{\bar{\jmath}}\right>
, the
effective Lagrangian can finally be rewritten as

•

For the single chiral matter multiplet case,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{F2}|_{q=p=0,k=n}\supset M_{pl}^{2(n-4)}\frac{\mathcal{U}^{(n)}}{\mathcal{F}^{2(4-n)}}\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\prime(2(6-n))}.

(7.195)

•

For two or more chiral matter multiplets,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{F6}|_{q=p=0,k=n}\supset c^{\prime}M_{pl}^{2(n-6)}\frac{\mathcal{U}^{(n)}}{\mathcal{F}^{2(6-n)}}\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\prime(2(8-n))}.

(7.196)

where
\mathcal{O}(10^{-2})\lesssim c^{\prime}\lesssim\mathcal{O}(1)
.

The effective operators we obtained are generically nonzero even after considering possible cancellations due to Fermi
statistics or nonlinear field redefinitions. As an example we can take terms containing
\chi^{i}
. They are made of two
composite
chiral multiplets
\chi^{W}
and
\chi^{T}
and these produce terms that do not vanish on shell (i.e. imposing
\cancel{\partial}P_{L}\chi^{I}\approx 0
for matter fermions).
For instance, in a theory with only one matter chiral multiplet
(z,P_{L}\chi,F)
, we have

W=-2K_{\bar{z}z}[\{(\cancel{\partial}z)^{2}-F^{*}\cancel{\partial}z\}(\bar{\chi}P_{R}\chi)+\{{F^{*}}^{2}-F^{*}\cancel{\partial}z\}(\bar{\chi}P_{L}\chi)]+2K_{\bar{z}z}K_{\bar{z}\bar{z}z}(\cancel{\partial}z-F^{*})(\bar{\chi}P_{L}\chi)(\bar{\chi}P_{R}\chi)
, and

\bar{W}=(W)^{*}
, so that
W\bar{W}=4K_{\bar{z}z}^{4}(|\cancel{\partial}z|^{2}+|F|^{2})||\cancel{\partial}z-F^{*}|^{2}(\bar{\chi}P_{L}\chi)(\bar{\chi}P_{R}\chi)
. Hence, looking at the possible fermionic terms from
\mathcal{L}_{F1}
, when
i=z,\bar{\jmath}=\bar{z}
(i.e.
q=p=m=0,k=n=2
), we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{F1}

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{2}\frac{\mathcal{U}^{(2)}}{\tilde{\mathcal{F}}^{4}}W\bar{W}(\partial_{\mu}z\partial^{\mu}\bar{z})=\Upsilon^{2}\frac{\mathcal{U}^{(2)}}{\tilde{\mathcal{F}}^{4}}4K_{\bar{z}z}^{4}(|\cancel{\partial}z|^{2}+|F|^{2})||\cancel{\partial}z-F^{*}|^{2}(\partial_{\mu}z\partial^{\mu}\bar{z})(\bar{\chi}P_{L}\chi)(\bar{\chi}P_{R}\chi).

It is easy to see that this operator does not vanish on the mass shell of the matter scalars,
\square z\approx 0
.
As another example, from
\mathcal{L}_{F2}
we get terms containing up to three matter fermions when we consider
q=m=p_{1}=0,k=n=1,p_{2}=p=1

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{F2}

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{2}\frac{\mathcal{U}^{(1)}}{\tilde{\mathcal{F}}^{4}}\frac{1}{2}W\bar{\chi}\cancel{\partial}P_{R}\chi^{\bar{W}}\approx\Upsilon^{2}\frac{\mathcal{U}^{(1)}}{\tilde{\mathcal{F}}^{4}}4K_{\bar{z}z}^{2}(\cancel{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{F}})(\cancel{\partial}z)(\bar{\chi}P_{R}\chi)P_{L}(\cancel{\partial}z-F^{*})^{2}\chi|_{\mbox{\small 3-fermion terms}}+\cdots.

Back to the results in Eqs. (
7.195
) and (
7.196
), the general effective Lagrangians can be cast in the form

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{F}=\Lambda_{cut}^{4-\delta}\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\prime(\delta)}=\begin{cases}M_{pl}^{2(n-4)}\dfrac{\mathcal{U}^{(n)}}{\mathcal{F}^{2(4-n)}}\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\prime(\delta=2(6-n))}~\qquad\textrm{for}~N_{mat}=1,\\
c^{\prime}M_{pl}^{2(n-6)}\dfrac{\mathcal{U}^{(n)}}{\mathcal{F}^{2(6-n)}}\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\prime(\delta=2(8-n))}~\qquad\textrm{for}~N_{mat}\geq 2.\end{cases}

(7.199)

where
\mathcal{O}(10^{-2})\lesssim c^{\prime}\lesssim\mathcal{O}(1)
;
N_{mat}
and
\Lambda_{cut}
are defined to be the number of chiral multiplets of matter, and the cutoff scale of our effective theory, respectively.

If we demand that our effective theory describe physics up to the energy scale
\Lambda_{cut}
we obtain the following
inequalities:

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}^{(n)}\lesssim\begin{cases}\mathcal{F}^{2(4-n)}\left(\dfrac{M_{pl}}{\Lambda_{cut}}\right)^{2(4-n)}\quad\textrm{where}\quad 0\leq n\leq 2\quad\textrm{for}~N_{mat}=1,\\
\mathcal{F}^{2(6-n)}\left(\dfrac{M_{pl}}{\Lambda_{cut}}\right)^{2(6-n)}\quad\textrm{where}\quad 0\leq n\leq 4\quad\textrm{for}~N_{mat}\geq 2.\end{cases}

(7.200)

A conventional definition of the supersymmetry breaking scale
M_{S}
is in terms of F-term expectation value so we define

M_{S}^{4}=M_{pl}^{4}\mathcal{F}
, so the constraints on
\mathcal{U}^{(n)}
become

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}^{(n)}\lesssim\begin{cases}\left(\dfrac{M_{S}}{M_{pl}}\right)^{8(4-n)}\left(\dfrac{M_{pl}}{\Lambda_{cut}}\right)^{2(4-n)}\quad\textrm{where}\quad 0\leq n\leq 2\quad\textrm{for}~N_{mat}=1,\\
\left(\dfrac{M_{S}}{M_{pl}}\right)^{8(6-n)}\left(\dfrac{M_{pl}}{\Lambda_{cut}}\right)^{2(6-n)}\quad\textrm{where}\quad 0\leq n\leq 4\quad\textrm{for}~N_{mat}\geq 2.\end{cases}

(7.201)

Equation (
7.201
) is the crucial one in our paper, as it constrains precisely the new
function
\mathcal{U}

introduced by liberated
\mathcal{N}=1
supergravity. The constraint depends on the reduced Planck scale
M_{pl}
, the
supersymmetry breaking scale
M_{S}
,
\Lambda_{cut}
and the number of chiral multiplets of matter in the theory.
Of course, when we push both the cutoff and supersymmetry breaking scales to the reduced Planck scale, i.e.
\Lambda_{cut}\sim M_{S}\sim M_{pl}
, we obtain a model-independent universal constraint

\displaystyle\forall n:~\mathcal{U}^{(n)}\lesssim 1.

(7.202)

A model where supersymmetry is broken at the Planck scale is hardly the most interesting. In the more interesting case that

M_{S}\ll M_{pl}
we need the constraints (
7.201
) again, so we need to first determine how many matter
chiral multiplets we have in our theory. The constraints will then depend only on our choice of

\Lambda_{cut}
and
M_{S}
.

In the rest of this section we will examine the constraints in two cases. The first is the true, post-inflationary vacuum of the
theory. To make a supergravity theory meaningful we want it to be valid at least
up to energies
\Lambda_{cut}\gtrsim M_{S}
.
The second is slow-roll inflation. In this case we must have
\Lambda_{cut}\gtrsim H
, with
H
the Hubble constant
during inflation.

For the post-inflationary vacuum the interesting regime is when
M_{S}
is relatively small, say

M_{S}\sim 10\,\mathrm{TeV}\approx 10^{-15}M_{pl}
and the effective theory is valid up to an energy scale not smaller
than
M_{S}
, i.e.
\Lambda_{cut}\gtrsim M_{S}
. If
\Lambda_{cut}<M_{S}
liberated supergravity would be a useless
complication, since in its domain of validity supersymmetry would be always nonlinearly realized.
In the post-inflationary vacuum, for the single matter chiral multiplet case, the constraints (
7.201
) thus give for

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}^{(0)}\lesssim\left(\frac{M_{S}}{M_{pl}}\right)^{32}\left(\frac{M_{pl}}{M_{S}}\right)^{8}\implies\mathcal{U}^{(0)}\sim 10^{-360},

(7.203)

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}^{(1)}\lesssim\left(\frac{M_{S}}{M_{pl}}\right)^{24}\left(\frac{M_{pl}}{M_{S}}\right)^{6}\implies\mathcal{U}^{(1)}\sim 10^{-270},

(7.204)

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}^{(2)}\lesssim\left(\frac{M_{S}}{M_{pl}}\right)^{16}\left(\frac{M_{pl}}{M_{S}}\right)^{4}\implies\mathcal{U}^{(2)}\sim 10^{-180}.

(7.205)

Notice that
\mathcal{U}^{(3)},\mathcal{U}^{(4)}
are not restricted. For two or more matter chiral multiplets, the
constraints are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}^{(0)}\lesssim\left(\frac{M_{S}}{M_{pl}}\right)^{48}\left(\frac{M_{pl}}{M_{S}}\right)^{12}\implies\mathcal{U}^{(0)}\sim 10^{-540},

(7.206)

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}^{(1)}\lesssim\left(\frac{M_{S}}{M_{pl}}\right)^{40}\left(\frac{M_{pl}}{M_{S}}\right)^{10}\implies\mathcal{U}^{(1)}\sim 10^{-450},

(7.207)

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}^{(2)}\lesssim\left(\frac{M_{S}}{M_{pl}}\right)^{32}\left(\frac{M_{pl}}{M_{S}}\right)^{8}\implies\mathcal{U}^{(2)}\sim 10^{-360}.

(7.208)

From the constraints on

\mathcal{U}^{(0)}
and
\mathcal{U}^{(2)}
, we find that the liberated scalar potential contributes only a negligibly small
cosmological constant and negligibly small corrections to the mass terms
of the chiral multiplet scalars. For the single chiral multiplet case, restoring dimensions we get a vacuum energy density

\mathcal{U}^{(0)}\lesssim 10^{-360}M_{pl}^{4}

(7.209)

and scalar masses

M_{z}\lesssim M_{pl}\sqrt{|\mathcal{U}^{(2)}|}=10^{-90}M_{pl}.

(7.210)

These constraints become even tighter if the theory contains more than one chiral multiplet, but the ones we obtained are
already so stringent as to rule out any observable contribution to the cosmological constant and scalar masses from the
new terms made possible by liberated supergravity. We can say that Eqs. (
7.209
) and (
7.210
) already send
liberated supergravity back to prison after the end of inflation.

The constraints during inflation instead can be easily satisfied if during inflation the supersymmetry breaking scale is
very high, say
M_{S}=M_{pl}
. In that case,
\mathcal{U}^{(0)}\lesssim\mathcal{O}(1)
.
After inflation the “worst case scenario” constraints coming from Eq. (
7.201
) with
N_{mat}\geq 2
and

M_{S}=10^{-15}M_{pl}
are

\displaystyle\forall n:~\mathcal{U}^{(n)}\lesssim 10^{-120(6-n)}.

(7.211)

A simple way to satisfy all these constraints is to choose a no scale structure for the supersymmetric part of the scalar potential. This ensures the vanishing of the F-term contribution to the potential independently of the magnitude of the
F-terms.

\displaystyle V_{F}=e^{G}(G_{I}G^{I\bar{\jmath}}G_{\bar{\jmath}}-3)=0,

(7.212)

The total scalar potential is then given by
V=V_{D}+V_{NEW}
. Thanks to the no-scale structure, we can have
both
M_{S}\sim M_{pl}
and
\mathcal{U}^{(0)}\sim H^{2}\sim 10^{-10}
during inflation.

Our scenario has
M_{S}=M_{pl}
during inflation and
M_{S}=10^{-15}M_{pl}
at the true vacuum in the post-inflation phase, so we see that to satisfy all constraints a transition between the two different epochs must occur, for which the scale of the composite F-term
\mathcal{F}
changes from
\mathcal{O}(M_{pl})
to
\mathcal{O}(10^{-15}M_{pl})
.

8

|
Revisiting New Kähler-Invariant Fayet-Iliopoulos Terms in the Superconformal Formalism

This chapter is based on the author’s original work in Ref.
[
67
]
.

8.1
Component Action of New FI Term in Superconformal Tensor Calculus

In this chapter, we review the component action of a new, Kähler invariant Fayet-Iliopoulos term proposed by Antoniadis, Chatrabhuti, Isono, and Knoops
[
54
]
, using again the superconformal tensor calculus. The full Lagrangian with the new FI terms is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-3[S_{0}\bar{S}_{0}e^{-K(z,\bar{z})}]_{D}+([S_{0}^{3}W(z)]_{F}-\frac{1}{4}[\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda]_{F}+h.c.)

(8.1)

\displaystyle-\xi\left[(S_{0}\bar{S}_{0}e^{-K(z,\bar{z})})^{-3}\frac{(\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda)(\bar{\lambda}P_{R}\lambda)}{T(\bar{w}^{\prime 2})\bar{T}(w^{\prime 2})}(V)_{D}\right]_{D},

where the last term coupled to the parameter
\xi
corresponds to the new FI terms.

We consider matter chiral multiplets
Z^{i}
, the chiral compensator
S_{0}
, a real multiplet
V
, and another real multiplet

(V)_{D}
, whose lowest component is the auxiliary D term of the real multiplet
V
. Their superconformal multiplets are given as follows:

\displaystyle V=\{0,0,0,0,A_{\mu},\lambda,D\}~\textrm{in the Wess-Zumino gauge,~i.e.}~v=\zeta=\mathcal{H}=0,

(8.2)

\displaystyle Z^{i}=(z^{i},-i\sqrt{2}P_{L}\chi^{i},-2F^{i},0,+i\mathcal{D}_{\mu}z^{i},0,0)=\{z^{i},P_{L}\chi^{i},F^{i}\},

(8.3)

\displaystyle\bar{Z}^{\bar{i}}=(\bar{z}^{\bar{i}},+i\sqrt{2}P_{R}\chi^{\bar{i}},0,-2\bar{F}^{\bar{i}},-i\mathcal{D}_{\mu}\bar{z}^{\bar{i}},0,0)=\{\bar{z}^{\bar{i}},P_{R}\chi^{\bar{i}},\bar{F}^{\bar{i}}\},

(8.4)

\displaystyle S_{0}=(s_{0},-i\sqrt{2}P_{L}\chi^{0},-2F_{0},0,+i\mathcal{D}_{\mu}s_{0},0,0)=\{s_{0},P_{L}\chi^{0},F_{0}\},

(8.5)

\displaystyle\bar{S}_{0}=(\bar{s}_{0},+i\sqrt{2}P_{R}\chi^{0},0,-2\bar{F}_{0},-i\mathcal{D}_{\mu}\bar{s}_{0},0,0)=\{\bar{s}_{0},P_{R}\chi^{0},\bar{F}_{0}\},

(8.6)

\displaystyle\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda=(\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda,-i\sqrt{2}P_{L}\Lambda,2D_{-}^{2},0,+i\mathcal{D}_{\mu}(\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda),0,0)=\{\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda,P_{L}\Lambda,-D_{-}^{2}\},

(8.7)

\displaystyle\bar{\lambda}P_{R}\lambda=(\bar{\lambda}P_{R}\lambda,+i\sqrt{2}P_{R}\Lambda,0,2D_{+}^{2},-i\mathcal{D}_{\mu}(\bar{\lambda}P_{R}\lambda),0,0)=\{\bar{\lambda}P_{R}\lambda,P_{R}\Lambda,-D_{+}^{2}\},

(8.8)

\displaystyle(V)_{D}=(D,\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\lambda,0,0,\mathcal{D}^{b}\hat{F}_{ab},-\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\lambda,-\square^{C}D),

(8.9)

where

\displaystyle P_{L}\Lambda\equiv\sqrt{2}P_{L}(-\frac{1}{2}\gamma\cdot\hat{F}+iD)\lambda,\qquad P_{R}\Lambda\equiv\sqrt{2}P_{R}(-\frac{1}{2}\gamma\cdot\hat{F}-iD)\lambda,

(8.10)

\displaystyle D_{-}^{2}\equiv D^{2}-\hat{F}^{-}\cdot\hat{F}^{-}-2\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\lambda,\qquad D_{+}^{2}\equiv D^{2}-\hat{F}^{+}\cdot\hat{F}^{+}-2\bar{\lambda}P_{R}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\lambda,

(8.11)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\mu}\lambda\equiv\bigg(\partial_{\mu}-\frac{3}{2}b_{\mu}+\frac{1}{4}w_{\mu}^{ab}\gamma_{ab}-\frac{3}{2}i\gamma_{*}\mathcal{A}_{\mu}\bigg)\lambda-\bigg(\frac{1}{4}\gamma^{ab}\hat{F}_{ab}+\frac{1}{2}i\gamma_{*}D\bigg)\psi_{\mu}

(8.12)

\displaystyle\hat{F}_{ab}\equiv F_{ab}+e_{a}^{~\mu}e_{b}^{~\nu}\bar{\psi}_{[\mu}\gamma_{\nu]}\lambda,\qquad F_{ab}\equiv e_{a}^{~\mu}e_{b}^{~\nu}(2\partial_{[\mu}A_{\nu]}),

(8.13)

\displaystyle\hat{F}^{\pm}_{\mu\nu}\equiv\frac{1}{2}(\hat{F}_{\mu\nu}\pm\tilde{\hat{F}}_{\mu\nu}),\qquad\tilde{\hat{F}}_{\mu\nu}\equiv-\frac{1}{2}i\epsilon_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\hat{F}^{\rho\sigma}.

(8.14)

8.1.1
w^{\prime 2},\bar{w}^{\prime 2}
Composite complex multiplets: (Weyl/Chiral) weights
=(-1,3)
and
(-1,-3)

We show here the components of the first superconformal
composite
complex multiplets
w^{\prime 2}
and
\bar{w}^{\prime 2}
with Weyl/chiral weights
(-1,3)
and
(-1,-3)
respectively. These composite multiplets are defined to be

\displaystyle w^{\prime 2}\equiv\frac{\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda}{(S_{0}\bar{S}_{0}e^{-K/3})^{2}}=\{\mathcal{C}_{w},\mathcal{Z}_{w},\mathcal{H}_{w},\mathcal{K}_{w},\mathcal{B}^{w}_{\mu},\Lambda_{w},\mathcal{D}_{w}\}

(8.15)

\displaystyle\bar{w}^{\prime 2}\equiv\frac{\bar{\lambda}P_{R}\lambda}{(S_{0}\bar{S}_{0}e^{-K/3})^{2}}=\{\mathcal{C}_{\bar{w}},\mathcal{Z}_{\bar{w}},\mathcal{H}_{\bar{w}},\mathcal{K}_{\bar{w}},\mathcal{B}^{\bar{w}}_{\mu},\Lambda_{\bar{w}},\mathcal{D}_{\bar{w}}\}.

(8.16)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{w}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h\equiv\frac{\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda}{(s_{0}\bar{s}_{0}e^{-K(z,\bar{z})/3})^{2}},

(8.17)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{w}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\sqrt{2}(-h_{a}\Omega^{a}+h_{\bar{a}}\Omega^{\bar{a}}),

(8.18)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{w}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2h_{a}F^{a}+h_{ab}\bar{\Omega}^{a}\Omega^{b},

(8.19)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{w}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2h_{\bar{a}}F^{\bar{a}}+h_{\bar{a}\bar{b}}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{a}}\Omega^{\bar{b}},

(8.20)

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{w}_{\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle ih_{a}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}X^{a}-ih_{\bar{a}}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}\bar{X}^{\bar{a}}+ih_{a\bar{b}}\bar{\Omega}^{a}\gamma_{\mu}\Omega^{\bar{b}},

(8.21)

\displaystyle P_{L}\Lambda_{w}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{2}ih_{\bar{a}b}[(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}X^{b})\Omega^{\bar{a}}-F^{\bar{a}}\Omega^{b}]-\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}h_{\bar{a}\bar{b}c}\Omega^{c}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{a}}\Omega^{\bar{b}},

(8.22)

\displaystyle P_{R}\Lambda_{w}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}ih_{a\bar{b}}[(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{X}^{\bar{b}})\Omega^{a}-F^{a}\Omega^{\bar{b}}]+\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}h_{ab\bar{c}}\Omega^{\bar{c}}\bar{\Omega}^{a}\Omega^{b},

(8.23)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{w}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2h_{a\bar{b}}\Big(-\mathcal{D}_{\mu}X^{a}\mathcal{D}^{\mu}\bar{X}^{\bar{b}}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Omega}^{a}P_{L}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\Omega^{\bar{b}}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{b}}P_{R}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\Omega^{a}+F^{a}F^{\bar{b}}\Big)

(8.24)

\displaystyle+h_{ab\bar{c}}(-\bar{\Omega}^{a}\Omega^{b}F^{\bar{c}}+\bar{\Omega}^{a}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}X^{b})\Omega^{\bar{c}})+h_{\bar{a}\bar{b}c}(-\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{a}}\Omega^{\bar{b}}F^{c}+\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{a}}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{X}^{\bar{b}})\Omega^{c})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}h_{ab\bar{c}\bar{d}}(\bar{\Omega}^{a}P_{L}\Omega^{b})(\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{c}}P_{R}\Omega^{\bar{d}}).

Notice that when finding the multiplet
\bar{w}^{\prime 2}
, we can just replace
h
by its complex conjugate
h^{*}
.

8.1.2
T(\bar{w}^{\prime 2}),\bar{T}(w^{\prime 2})
chiral projection multiplets: (Weyl/Chiral) weights
=(0,0)

The second superconformal multiplets that we need are the
composite
chiral projection multiplets
T(\bar{w}^{\prime 2})
and
\bar{T}(w^{\prime 2})
with Weyl/chiral weights
(0,0)
. From their component supermultiplets defined by

\displaystyle T(\bar{w}^{\prime 2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{K}_{\bar{w}},-\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{2}iP_{L}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\mathcal{Z}_{\bar{w}}+\Lambda_{\bar{w}}),\frac{1}{2}(\mathcal{D}_{\bar{w}}+\square^{C}\mathcal{C}_{\bar{w}}+i\mathcal{D}_{a}\mathcal{B}^{a}_{\bar{w}})\right),

(8.25)

\displaystyle\bar{T}(w^{\prime 2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{K}_{\bar{w}}^{*},\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{2}iP_{R}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\mathcal{Z}_{\bar{w}}^{C}+\Lambda_{\bar{w}}^{C}),\frac{1}{2}(\mathcal{D}_{\bar{w}}^{*}+\square^{C}\mathcal{C}_{\bar{w}}^{*}-i\mathcal{D}_{a}(\mathcal{B}^{a}_{\bar{w}})^{*})\right)

(8.26)

we find the corresponding superconformal multiplets and their complex conjugates as follows:

\displaystyle T\equiv T(\bar{w}^{\prime 2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{\mathcal{C}_{T},\mathcal{Z}_{T},\mathcal{H}_{T},\mathcal{K}_{T},\mathcal{B}_{\mu}^{T},\Lambda_{T},\mathcal{D}_{T}\}

\displaystyle\bar{T}\equiv\bar{T}(w^{\prime 2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{\mathcal{C}_{\bar{T}},\mathcal{Z}_{\bar{T}},\mathcal{H}_{\bar{T}},\mathcal{K}_{\bar{T}},\mathcal{B}_{\mu}^{\bar{T}},\Lambda_{\bar{T}},\mathcal{D}_{\bar{T}}\},

(8.27)

whose superconformal components are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{K}_{\bar{w}}=h^{*}_{\bar{a}}F^{\bar{a}}-\frac{1}{2}h^{*}_{\bar{a}\bar{b}}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{a}}\Omega^{\bar{b}}\equiv C_{T}

(8.28)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{2}iP_{L}\bigg[\cancel{\mathcal{D}}(-h^{*}_{a}\Omega^{a}+h^{*}_{\bar{a}}\Omega^{\bar{a}})-h^{*}_{\bar{a}b}[(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}X^{b})\Omega^{\bar{a}}-F^{\bar{a}}\Omega^{b}]-\frac{1}{2}h^{*}_{\bar{a}\bar{b}c}\Omega^{c}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{a}}\Omega^{\bar{b}}

(8.29)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+h^{*}_{a\bar{b}}[(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{X}^{\bar{b}})\Omega^{a}-F^{a}\Omega^{\bar{b}}]+\frac{1}{2}h^{*}_{ab\bar{c}}\Omega^{\bar{c}}\bar{\Omega}^{a}\Omega^{b}\bigg]\equiv-\sqrt{2}iP_{L}\Omega_{T},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\bigg[h^{*}_{a\bar{b}}\Big(-\mathcal{D}_{\mu}X^{a}\mathcal{D}^{\mu}\bar{X}^{\bar{b}}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Omega}^{a}P_{L}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\Omega^{\bar{b}}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{b}}P_{R}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\Omega^{a}+F^{a}F^{\bar{b}}\Big)

(8.30)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}h^{*}_{ab\bar{c}}(-\bar{\Omega}^{a}\Omega^{b}F^{\bar{c}}+\bar{\Omega}^{a}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}X^{b})\Omega^{\bar{c}})+\frac{1}{2}h^{*}_{\bar{a}\bar{b}c}(-\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{a}}\Omega^{\bar{b}}F^{c}+\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{a}}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{X}^{\bar{b}})\Omega^{c})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}h^{*}_{ab\bar{c}\bar{d}}(\bar{\Omega}^{a}P_{L}\Omega^{b})(\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{c}}P_{R}\Omega^{\bar{d}})+\frac{1}{2}\square^{C}h^{*}+\frac{1}{2}i\mathcal{D}^{\mu}(ih^{*}_{a}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}X^{a}-ih^{*}_{\bar{a}}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}\bar{X}^{\bar{a}}+ih^{*}_{a\bar{b}}\bar{\Omega}^{a}\gamma_{\mu}\Omega^{\bar{b}})\bigg]

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle-2F_{T},

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0,

(8.31)

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{T}_{\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i\mathcal{D}_{\mu}\mathcal{C}_{T},

(8.32)

\displaystyle\Lambda_{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0,

(8.33)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0,

(8.34)

where we used
a,b,c,d=0,i(\equiv z^{i}),W(\equiv\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda)
. This gives the superfield components of the chiral projection multiplet
T
:

\displaystyle T(\bar{w}^{\prime 2})=(C_{T},P_{L}\Omega_{T},F_{T})

(8.35)

where

\displaystyle C_{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h^{*}_{\bar{a}}F^{\bar{a}}-\frac{1}{2}h^{*}_{\bar{a}\bar{b}}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{a}}\Omega^{\bar{b}},

(8.36)

\displaystyle P_{L}\Omega_{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\cancel{\mathcal{D}}(-h^{*}_{a}\Omega^{a}+h^{*}_{\bar{a}}\Omega^{\bar{a}})-h^{*}_{\bar{a}b}[(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}X^{b})\Omega^{\bar{a}}-F^{\bar{a}}\Omega^{b}]-\frac{1}{2}h^{*}_{\bar{a}\bar{b}c}\Omega^{c}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{a}}\Omega^{\bar{b}}

(8.37)

\displaystyle+h^{*}_{a\bar{b}}[(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{X}^{\bar{b}})\Omega^{a}-F^{a}\Omega^{\bar{b}}]+\frac{1}{2}h^{*}_{ab\bar{c}}\Omega^{\bar{c}}\bar{\Omega}^{a}\Omega^{b},

\displaystyle F_{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h^{*}_{a\bar{b}}\Big(-\mathcal{D}_{\mu}X^{a}\mathcal{D}^{\mu}\bar{X}^{\bar{b}}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Omega}^{a}P_{L}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\Omega^{\bar{b}}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{b}}P_{R}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\Omega^{a}+F^{a}F^{\bar{b}}\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}h^{*}_{ab\bar{c}}(-\bar{\Omega}^{a}\Omega^{b}F^{\bar{c}}+\bar{\Omega}^{a}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}X^{b})\Omega^{\bar{c}})+\frac{1}{2}h^{*}_{\bar{a}\bar{b}c}(-\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{a}}\Omega^{\bar{b}}F^{c}+\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{a}}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{X}^{\bar{b}})\Omega^{c})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}h^{*}_{ab\bar{c}\bar{d}}(\bar{\Omega}^{a}P_{L}\Omega^{b})(\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{c}}P_{R}\Omega^{\bar{d}})+\frac{1}{2}\square^{C}h^{*}-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{D}^{\mu}(h^{*}_{a}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}X^{a}-h^{*}_{\bar{a}}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}\bar{X}^{\bar{a}}+h^{*}_{a\bar{b}}\bar{\Omega}^{a}\gamma_{\mu}\Omega^{\bar{b}}).

Morever,

\displaystyle\bar{T}(w^{\prime 2})=\{C_{T}^{*},P_{R}\Omega_{T},F_{T}^{*}\}

(8.39)

where

\displaystyle C_{T}^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h_{a}F^{a}-\frac{1}{2}h_{ab}\bar{\Omega}^{a}\Omega^{b},

(8.40)

\displaystyle P_{R}\Omega_{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\cancel{\mathcal{D}}(-h_{a}\Omega^{a}+h_{\bar{a}}\Omega^{\bar{a}})-h_{\bar{a}b}[(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}X^{b})\Omega^{\bar{a}}-F^{\bar{a}}\Omega^{b}]-\frac{1}{2}h_{\bar{a}\bar{b}c}\Omega^{c}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{a}}\Omega^{\bar{b}}

(8.41)

\displaystyle+h_{a\bar{b}}[(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{X}^{\bar{b}})\Omega^{a}-F^{a}\Omega^{\bar{b}}]+\frac{1}{2}h_{ab\bar{c}}\Omega^{\bar{c}}\bar{\Omega}^{a}\Omega^{b},

\displaystyle F_{T}^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h_{a\bar{b}}\Big(-\mathcal{D}_{\mu}X^{a}\mathcal{D}^{\mu}\bar{X}^{\bar{b}}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Omega}^{a}P_{L}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\Omega^{\bar{b}}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{b}}P_{R}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\Omega^{a}+F^{a}F^{\bar{b}}\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}h_{ab\bar{c}}(-\bar{\Omega}^{a}\Omega^{b}F^{\bar{c}}+\bar{\Omega}^{a}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}X^{b})\Omega^{\bar{c}})+\frac{1}{2}h_{\bar{a}\bar{b}c}(-\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{a}}\Omega^{\bar{b}}F^{c}+\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{a}}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{X}^{\bar{b}})\Omega^{c})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}h_{ab\bar{c}\bar{d}}(\bar{\Omega}^{a}P_{L}\Omega^{b})(\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{c}}P_{R}\Omega^{\bar{d}})+\frac{1}{2}\square^{C}h-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{D}^{\mu}(h_{a}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}X^{a}-h_{\bar{a}}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}\bar{X}^{\bar{a}}+h_{a\bar{b}}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{b}}\gamma_{\mu}\Omega^{a}).

8.1.3
Composite real multiplet
\mathcal{R}
: (Weyl/Chiral) weights
=(0,0)

We present here a superconformal composite real multiplet
\mathcal{R}
with Weyl/chiral weights
(0,0)
. Defining some chiral multiplets
\mathcal{X}^{A}\equiv\{X^{A},P_{L}\Omega^{A},F^{A}\}
where
A=\{S_{0},Z^{i},\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda,T(\bar{w}^{\prime 2})
} and their conjugates, we represent the composite one
\mathcal{R}
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}\equiv(S_{0}\bar{S}_{0}e^{-K/3})^{-3}\frac{(\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda)(\bar{\lambda}P_{R}\lambda)}{T(\bar{w}^{\prime 2})\bar{T}(w^{\prime 2})}

(8.43)

whose lowest component is

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{R}}\equiv(s_{0}\bar{s}_{0}e^{-K/3})^{-3}\frac{(\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda)(\bar{\lambda}P_{R}\lambda)}{C_{T}C_{\bar{T}}}\equiv f(X^{A},\bar{X}^{\bar{A}})

(8.44)

where
C_{T}=-D_{+}^{2}\Delta^{-2}
;
C_{\bar{T}}=-D_{-}^{2}\Delta^{-2}
, and
\Delta\equiv s_{0}\bar{s}_{0}e^{-K/3}
. Then, the superconformal multiplet of the new Fayet-Iliopoulos term can be written by using

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}\cdot(V)_{D}=\{\tilde{\mathcal{C}},\tilde{\mathcal{Z}},\tilde{\mathcal{H}},\tilde{\mathcal{K}},\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\mu},\tilde{\Lambda},\tilde{\mathcal{D}}\},

(8.45)

whose superconformal multiplet components are as follows:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{C}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Df,

(8.46)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\lambda+Di\sqrt{2}(-f_{A}\Omega^{A}+f_{\bar{A}}\Omega^{\bar{A}}),

(8.47)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D(-2f_{A}F^{A}+f_{AB}\bar{\Omega}^{A}\Omega^{B})-i\sqrt{2}(-f_{A}\bar{\Omega}^{A}+f_{\bar{A}}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{A}})P_{L}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\lambda,

(8.48)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{K}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D(-2f_{\bar{A}}F^{\bar{A}}+f_{\bar{A}\bar{B}}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{A}}\Omega^{\bar{B}})-i\sqrt{2}(-f_{A}\bar{\Omega}^{A}+f_{\bar{A}}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{A}})P_{R}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\lambda,

(8.49)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{B}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\mathcal{D}^{\nu}\hat{F}_{\mu\nu})f+D(if_{A}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}X^{A}-if_{\bar{A}}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}\bar{X}^{\bar{A}}+if_{A\bar{B}}\bar{\Omega}^{A}\gamma_{\mu}\Omega^{\bar{B}}),

(8.50)

\displaystyle\tilde{\Lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-f\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\lambda+D(P_{L}\Lambda^{f}+P_{R}\Lambda^{f})+\frac{1}{2}\Big(\gamma_{*}(-f_{A}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}X^{A}+f_{\bar{A}}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{X}^{\bar{A}}-f_{A\bar{B}}\bar{\Omega}^{A}\cancel{\gamma}\Omega^{\bar{B}})

(8.51)

\displaystyle+P_{L}(-2f_{\bar{A}}F^{\bar{A}}+f_{\bar{A}\bar{B}}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{A}}\Omega^{\bar{B}})+P_{R}(-2f_{A}F^{A}+f_{AB}\bar{\Omega}^{A}\Omega^{B})-\cancel{\mathcal{D}}f\Big)\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\lambda

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\Big(i\gamma_{*}\gamma^{\mu}\mathcal{D}^{\nu}\hat{F}_{\mu\nu}-\cancel{\mathcal{D}}D\Big)i\sqrt{2}(-f_{A}\Omega^{A}+f_{\bar{A}}\Omega^{\bar{A}}),

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{D}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-f\square^{C}D+D\bigg\{2f_{A\bar{B}}(-\mathcal{D}_{\mu}X^{A}\mathcal{D}^{\mu}\bar{X}^{\bar{B}}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Omega}^{A}P_{L}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\Omega^{\bar{B}}-\frac{1}{2}\cancel{\Omega}^{\bar{B}}P_{R}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\Omega^{A}+F^{A}F^{\bar{B}})

\displaystyle+f_{AB\bar{C}}(-\bar{\Omega}^{A}\Omega^{B}F^{\bar{C}}+\bar{\Omega}^{A}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}X^{B})\Omega^{\bar{C}})+f_{\bar{A}\bar{B}C}(-\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{A}}\Omega^{\bar{B}}F^{C}+\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{A}}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{X}^{\bar{B}})\Omega^{C})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}f_{AB\bar{C}\bar{D}}(\bar{\Omega}^{A}P_{L}\Omega^{B})(\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{C}}P_{R}\Omega^{\bar{D}})\bigg\}

\displaystyle-(\mathcal{D}_{\nu}\hat{F}^{\mu\nu})(if_{A}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}X^{A}-if_{\bar{A}}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}\bar{X}^{\bar{A}}+if_{A\bar{B}}\bar{\Omega}^{A}\gamma_{\mu}\Omega^{\bar{B}})

\displaystyle+\bigg(\sqrt{2}if_{\bar{A}B}[(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}X^{B})\Omega^{\bar{A}}-F^{\bar{A}}\Omega^{B}]+\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}f_{\bar{A}\bar{B}C}\Omega^{C}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{A}}\Omega^{\bar{B}}\bigg)\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\lambda

\displaystyle-\bigg(\sqrt{2}if_{A\bar{B}}[(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{X}^{\bar{B}})\Omega^{A}-F^{A}\Omega^{\bar{B}}]+\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}f_{AB\bar{C}}\Omega^{\bar{C}}\bar{\Omega}^{A}\Omega^{B}\bigg)\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\lambda

\displaystyle-(\mathcal{D}_{\mu}f)(\mathcal{D}^{\mu}D)-\frac{1}{2}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}[i\sqrt{2}(-f_{A}\Omega^{A}+f_{\bar{A}}\Omega^{\bar{A}})](\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\lambda)+\frac{1}{2}i\sqrt{2}(-f_{A}\Omega^{A}+f_{\bar{A}}\Omega^{\bar{A}})(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\lambda),

where the indices
A,B,C,D
run over
0,i,W,T
. The component action of the new FI term is then given by the D-term density formula

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{NEW}\equiv-[\xi\mathcal{R}\cdot(V)_{D}]_{D}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\xi}{4}\int d^{4}xe\bigg[\tilde{\mathcal{D}}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\psi}\cdot\gamma i\gamma_{*}\tilde{\Lambda}-\frac{1}{3}\tilde{\mathcal{C}}R(\omega)

(8.53)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{6}\Big(\tilde{\mathcal{C}}\bar{\psi}_{\mu}\gamma^{\mu\rho\sigma}-i\bar{\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}}\gamma^{\rho\sigma}\gamma_{*}\Big)R^{\prime}_{\rho\sigma}(Q)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}\varepsilon^{abcd}\bar{\psi}_{a}\gamma_{b}\psi_{c}\Big(\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{d}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\psi}_{d}\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}\Big)\bigg]+\textrm{h.c.}.

8.1.4
Bosonic term of the new FI term

The new FI term is obtained from the term
D^{2}f_{W\bar{W}}F^{W}F^{\bar{W}}
inside the D-term
\tilde{\mathcal{D}}
. Thus, we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{NEW}

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle-\xi Df_{W\bar{W}}F^{W}F^{\bar{W}}=-\xi D\frac{(s_{0}\bar{s}_{0}e^{-K/3})^{-3}}{C_{T}C_{T}^{*}}F^{W}F^{\bar{W}}\supset-\xi D\frac{(s_{0}\bar{s}_{0}e^{-K/3})^{-3}}{(h_{W}F^{W})(h^{*}_{\bar{W}}F^{\bar{W}})}F^{W}F^{\bar{W}}

(8.54)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\xi D\frac{(s_{0}\bar{s}_{0}e^{-K/3})^{-3}}{(s_{0}\bar{s}_{0}e^{-K/3})^{-4}F^{W}F^{\bar{W}}}F^{W}F^{\bar{W}}=-\xi D(s_{0}\bar{s}_{0}e^{-K/3}).

Hence, in the superconformal gauge (
s_{0}\bar{s}_{0}e^{-K/3}=M_{pl}^{2}=1
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\textrm{new FI}}/e=-\xi D,

(8.55)

or

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\textrm{new FI}}/e=-M_{pl}^{2}\xi D.

(8.56)

8.2
Spectroscophy for Non-renormalizable Interactions in the New FI Terms

In this section, we carefully analyze suppression of the nonrenormalizable fermionic interactions from the new FI term from the perspective of effective field theory. To do this, as we did in the previous section of the liberated supergravity, we also need to recall the EFT expansion reviewed in Sec.
3.4
, and consider the structure of EFT expansion in Eq. (
3.18
) and constraint given in Eq. (
3.19
). Hence, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{EFT}\supset\sum_{\delta\geq 0}^{\textrm{Finite N}}\frac{C^{\delta}}{M^{\delta-d}}\mathcal{O}^{(\delta)}\sim\sum_{\delta\geq 0}^{\textrm{Finite N}}\frac{1}{\Lambda_{cut}^{\delta-d}}\mathcal{O}^{(\delta)}\implies\frac{C^{\delta}}{M^{\delta-d}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\Lambda_{cut}^{\delta-d}},

(8.57)

where
\Lambda_{cut}
is a cutoff scale;
M
is a characteristic mass scale of a theory;
C^{\delta}
is a dimensionless Wilson coefficient, and
\mathcal{O}^{(\delta)}
is an effective field operator with the mass dimension
\delta
. In the case of the new FI terms, we will see that the constraint depends on the nw FI terms
\xi
and thus the auxiliary field
D
of the vector multiplet. The key equation we will see in this section is given by

\displaystyle\Lambda_{cut}\lesssim H^{\alpha},

where
H
is the Hubble scale, and
\alpha
is a parameter that depends on the mass dimension of the effective field operator. Since
\Lambda_{cut},~H\sim 10^{-5}M_{pl}<M_{pl}=1
, we explore whether
\alpha<1
holds. After all, we will find that the maximum of
\alpha
is 2/3.

In particular, for the later use, we generalize the new FI constant
\xi
into a real gauge-invariant function
\mathcal{U}(z,\bar{z})
of matter fields. Thus, for ACIK FI term, we can just put
\mathcal{U}(z,\bar{z})\equiv\xi
. First, after solving the equations of motion for the auxiliary fields and fixing the superconformal gauge, the component multiplets “on-shell” are given by

\displaystyle Z^{i}=\{z^{i},P_{L}\Omega^{i},F^{i}\},

(8.58)

\displaystyle S_{0}=\{s_{0},\quad\frac{1}{3}e^{K/6}K_{i}P_{L}\Omega^{i},\quad F_{0}\},

(8.59)

\displaystyle W\equiv\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda=\Big\{\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda,\quad\sqrt{2}P_{L}(-\frac{1}{2}\gamma\cdot\hat{F}+iD)\lambda,\quad-D^{2}+\hat{F}^{-}\cdot\hat{F}^{-}\Big\},

(8.60)

where

\displaystyle\hat{F}_{ab}\equiv F_{ab}+e_{a}^{~\mu}e_{b}^{~\nu}\bar{\psi}_{[\mu}\gamma_{\nu]}\lambda,\qquad F_{ab}\equiv e_{a}^{~\mu}e_{b}^{~\nu}(2\partial_{[\mu}A_{\nu]}),

(8.61)

\displaystyle\hat{F}^{\pm}_{\mu\nu}\equiv\frac{1}{2}(\hat{F}_{\mu\nu}\pm\tilde{\hat{F}}_{\mu\nu}),\qquad\tilde{\hat{F}}_{\mu\nu}\equiv-\frac{1}{2}i\epsilon_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\hat{F}^{\rho\sigma},

(8.62)

and the solutions for the auxiliary fields are identified as

\displaystyle D

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}+\frac{1}{\mathcal{U}^{2}}\bigg[\bigg(-F^{0}\mathcal{U}e^{-K/6}+\mathcal{U}_{I}F^{I}+\frac{1}{3}\mathcal{U}K_{I}F^{I}\bigg)(\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda)-i\frac{\mathcal{U}_{I}}{\sqrt{2}}(\bar{\Omega}^{I}P_{L}\lambda)+h.c.\bigg]

(8.63)

\displaystyle+\textrm{higher order terms},

\displaystyle F^{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{2K/3}\bar{W}-\frac{1}{3}e^{K/6}(\bar{\lambda}P_{R}\lambda),

(8.64)

\displaystyle F^{\bar{J}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-3e^{K/2}G^{I\bar{J}}\nabla_{I}W-G^{I\bar{J}}\Big(9\frac{\mathcal{U}_{I}}{\mathcal{U}}+3K_{I}\Big)(\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda)

(8.65)

Plus, the relevant chiral projection multiplet
T(\bar{w}^{\prime 2})
is given by

\displaystyle T(\bar{w}^{\prime 2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(C_{T},P_{L}\Omega_{T},F_{T}),

(8.66)

\displaystyle C_{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h^{*}_{\bar{a}}F^{\bar{a}}-\frac{1}{2}h^{*}_{\bar{a}\bar{b}}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{a}}\Omega^{\bar{b}},

(8.67)

\displaystyle P_{L}\Omega_{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\cancel{\mathcal{D}}(-h^{*}_{a}\Omega^{a}+h^{*}_{\bar{a}}\Omega^{\bar{a}})-h^{*}_{\bar{a}b}[(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}X^{b})\Omega^{\bar{a}}-F^{\bar{a}}\Omega^{b}]-\frac{1}{2}h^{*}_{\bar{a}\bar{b}c}\Omega^{c}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{a}}\Omega^{\bar{b}}

(8.68)

\displaystyle+h^{*}_{a\bar{b}}[(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{X}^{\bar{b}})\Omega^{a}-F^{a}\Omega^{\bar{b}}]+\frac{1}{2}h^{*}_{ab\bar{c}}\Omega^{\bar{c}}\bar{\Omega}^{a}\Omega^{b},

\displaystyle F_{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h^{*}_{a\bar{b}}\Big(-\mathcal{D}_{\mu}X^{a}\mathcal{D}^{\mu}\bar{X}^{\bar{b}}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Omega}^{a}P_{L}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\Omega^{\bar{b}}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{b}}P_{R}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\Omega^{a}+F^{a}F^{\bar{b}}\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}h^{*}_{ab\bar{c}}(-\bar{\Omega}^{a}\Omega^{b}F^{\bar{c}}+\bar{\Omega}^{a}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}X^{b})\Omega^{\bar{c}})+\frac{1}{2}h^{*}_{\bar{a}\bar{b}c}(-\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{a}}\Omega^{\bar{b}}F^{c}+\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{a}}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{X}^{\bar{b}})\Omega^{c})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}h^{*}_{ab\bar{c}\bar{d}}(\bar{\Omega}^{a}P_{L}\Omega^{b})(\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{c}}P_{R}\Omega^{\bar{d}})+\frac{1}{2}\square^{C}h^{*}-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{D}^{\mu}(h^{*}_{a}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}X^{a}-h^{*}_{\bar{a}}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}\bar{X}^{\bar{a}}+h^{*}_{a\bar{b}}\bar{\Omega}^{a}\gamma_{\mu}\Omega^{\bar{b}}),

where
C_{T},C_{\bar{T}}=-D^{2}\Delta^{-2}+\textrm{fermions including }\lambda
;
\Delta\equiv s_{0}\bar{s}_{0}e^{-K/3}
;
a=0,i,W(=\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda)
, and

\displaystyle h^{*}\equiv\frac{\bar{\lambda}P_{R}\lambda}{(s_{0}\bar{s}_{0}e^{-K(z,\bar{z})/3})^{2}}.

(8.70)

We note that in this chiral projection multiplet, the terms “
h^{*}_{\bar{W}}\bar{F}^{\bar{W}}
of
C_{T}
,” “
h^{*}_{\bar{W}k}\bar{F}^{\bar{W}}\Omega^{k}
of
P_{L}\Omega_{T}
,” and “
F^{k}\bar{F}^{\bar{W}}
and
h^{*}_{jk\bar{W}}\bar{F}^{\bar{W}}\bar{\Omega}^{j}\Omega^{k}
of
F_{T}
” (where
j,k=0,i
) have no
\lambda
, while the other terms must have at least one
\lambda
.

In summary, the components of the multiplets can be found to be

\displaystyle S_{0}:\quad s_{0}\sim e^{K/6},\quad\cancel{\mathcal{D}}s_{0}\sim K_{i}\mathcal{O}_{i}^{(2)}+\mathcal{O}^{(1)}+K_{i}\mathcal{O}^{(4)}_{i\bar{\psi}},

\displaystyle\qquad P_{L}\Omega^{0}\sim K_{i}e^{K/6}\mathcal{O}^{(3/2)}_{i},\quad F^{0}\sim We^{2K/3},

(8.71)

\displaystyle Z^{i}:\quad z^{i}\sim z^{i},\quad\cancel{\mathcal{D}}z^{i}\sim\mathcal{O}^{(2)}+\mathcal{O}^{(4)}_{\bar{\psi}},\quad P_{L}\Omega^{i}\sim\mathcal{O}^{(3/2)}_{i},\quad F^{i}\sim F^{i},

(8.72)

\displaystyle W:\quad(\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda)\sim\mathcal{O}^{(3)}_{\lambda\lambda},\quad\cancel{\mathcal{D}}(\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda)\sim\mathcal{O}^{(4)}_{\bar{\lambda}\lambda}+\mathcal{O}^{(5)}_{\bar{\psi}\lambda}+D\mathcal{O}^{(3)}_{\bar{\psi}\lambda},
