\displaystyle\delta_{S}(\eta)\delta_{Q}(\epsilon)\mathcal{C}=\delta_{S}(\eta)\Big(\frac{i}{2}\bar{\epsilon}\gamma_{*}\mathcal{Z}\Big)=\frac{i}{2}\bar{\epsilon}\gamma_{*}[\delta_{S}(\eta)\mathcal{Z}].

(B.53)

The right-hand side of Eq. (
B.52
) gives

\displaystyle[\delta_{S}(\eta),\delta_{Q}(\epsilon)]\mathcal{C}+\delta_{Q}(\epsilon)\delta_{S}(\eta)\mathcal{C}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{D}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\bar{\eta}\epsilon\Big)\mathcal{C}+\delta_{M}\Big(\frac{1}{4}\bar{\epsilon}\gamma^{ab}\eta\Big)\mathcal{C}+\delta_{A}\Big(-\frac{i}{2}\bar{\epsilon}\gamma_{*}\eta\Big)\mathcal{C}

(B.54)

\displaystyle+\delta_{Q}(\epsilon)\delta_{S}(\eta)\mathcal{C}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\bar{\eta}\epsilon w\mathcal{C}+0-ic\frac{i}{2}\bar{\epsilon}\gamma_{*}\eta\mathcal{C}+0=\frac{1}{2}\bar{\epsilon}\gamma_{*}^{2}\eta w\mathcal{C}-ic\frac{i}{2}\bar{\epsilon}\gamma_{*}\eta\mathcal{C}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2}\bar{\epsilon}\gamma_{*}\Big(-iw\gamma_{*}-ic\Big)\eta\mathcal{C},

where we used
\gamma_{*}^{2}=1
, which defines the projection operators
P_{L}\equiv\frac{1+\gamma_{*}}{2}
and
P_{R}\equiv\frac{1-\gamma_{*}}{2}
. By equating these two results, we can determine the
S
-transformation of the new field
\mathcal{Z}
we introduced in Eq. (
B.50
):

\displaystyle\delta_{S}(\eta)\mathcal{Z}=-i(w\gamma_{*}+c)\eta\mathcal{C}.

(B.55)

The point here is that
transformation laws of a descendant field can be uniquely determined by the transformation laws of the ascendant fields
. In particular, we can also take the decomposition of spinor
\psi=P_{L}\psi+P_{R}\psi
using the projection operators
P_{L}\equiv(1+\gamma_{*})/2,P_{R}\equiv(1-\gamma_{*})/2
such that
P_{L}^{2}=P_{R}^{2}=1,~P_{L}P_{R}=P_{R}P_{L}=0,~\gamma_{*}P_{L}=P_{L},~\gamma_{*}P_{R}=-P_{R}
. Thus, we can also get

\displaystyle\delta_{S}(\eta)P_{L}\mathcal{Z}=-i(w+c)P_{L}\eta\mathcal{C},\qquad\delta_{S}(\eta)P_{R}\mathcal{Z}=i(w-c)P_{R}\eta\mathcal{C}.

(B.56)

Another important computation is the
Q
-transformation of
\mathcal{Z}
. Let us consider

\displaystyle[\delta_{Q}(\epsilon_{1}),\delta_{Q}(\epsilon_{2})]\mathcal{C}=-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\epsilon}_{1}\gamma^{a}\epsilon_{2}\mathcal{D}_{a}\mathcal{C},

(B.57)

where
\mathcal{D}_{a}
is a relevant covariant derivative. To calculate the left-hand side of this commutator, let us consider the most general form of
\delta_{Q}\mathcal{Z}
. In the basis of
\gamma
-matrices, we can express
\delta_{Q}\mathcal{Z}
as

\displaystyle\delta_{Q}\mathcal{Z}=(\Phi^{0}+\Phi^{1}\gamma^{a}+\Phi^{2}_{ab}\gamma^{ab}+\gamma^{a}\gamma_{*}\Phi^{3}_{a}+\gamma_{*}\Phi^{4})\epsilon,

(B.58)

\displaystyle\delta_{Q}P_{L}\mathcal{Z}=P_{L}(\Phi^{0}+\Phi^{1}\gamma^{a}+\Phi^{2}_{ab}\gamma^{ab}-\gamma^{a}\Phi^{3}_{a}+\Phi^{4})\epsilon,

(B.59)

\displaystyle\delta_{Q}P_{R}\mathcal{Z}=P_{R}(\Phi^{0}+\Phi^{1}\gamma^{a}+\Phi^{2}_{ab}\gamma^{ab}+\gamma^{a}\Phi^{3}_{a}-\Phi^{4})\epsilon,

(B.60)

where
\Phi^{A}
(
A=0,1,2,3,4
) are bosonic fields with(out) the local Lorentz indices, and we used
P_{L/R}\gamma^{a}=\gamma^{a}P_{R/L}
given by
\{\gamma^{a},\gamma_{*}\}=0
. In fact, the expansion coefficients,
\Phi^{I}
’s, correspond to all the possible supersymmetric descendant fields of
\mathcal{Z}
. Then, the commutator result can be given by

\displaystyle[\delta_{Q}(\epsilon_{1}),\delta_{Q}(\epsilon_{2})]\mathcal{C}=\frac{i}{2}\bar{\epsilon}_{2}\gamma_{*}\delta_{Q}(\epsilon_{1})\mathcal{Z}-(\epsilon_{1}\leftrightarrow\epsilon_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{i}{2}(\bar{\epsilon}_{2}\gamma_{*}\epsilon_{1}\Phi^{0}+\bar{\epsilon}_{2}\gamma_{*}\gamma^{a}\epsilon_{1}\Phi_{a}^{1}+\bar{\epsilon}_{2}\gamma_{*}\gamma^{ab}\epsilon_{1}\Phi_{ab}^{2}+\bar{\epsilon}_{2}\gamma_{*}\gamma^{a}\gamma_{*}\epsilon_{1}\Phi_{a}^{3}+\bar{\epsilon}_{2}\epsilon_{1}\Phi^{4})-(\epsilon_{1}\leftrightarrow\epsilon_{2})\qquad

\displaystyle=i\bar{\epsilon}_{2}\gamma_{*}\gamma^{ab}\epsilon_{1}\Phi_{ab}^{2}+i\bar{\epsilon}_{1}\gamma^{a}\epsilon_{2}\Phi_{a}^{3}\overset{!}{=}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\epsilon}_{1}\gamma^{a}\epsilon_{2}\mathcal{D}_{a}\mathcal{C}

\displaystyle\implies\therefore~\Phi_{ab}^{2}=0\textrm{~~and~~}\Phi_{a}^{3}\equiv\frac{i}{2}\mathcal{D}_{a}\mathcal{C}

(B.61)

The undetermined coefficients
\Phi^{0},\Phi_{a}^{1},\Phi^{4}
will be considered as the supersymmetric descendant fields of
\mathcal{Z}
. Conventionally, we represent them as (following the notation of FKVW
[
70
]
1
1

1

The Kugo notation
H,K
can be written in terms of our notation as
H\equiv\frac{1}{2}(\mathcal{H}+\mathcal{K})
and
K\equiv\frac{i}{2}(\mathcal{K}-\mathcal{H})
.
):

\displaystyle\Phi^{0}\equiv-\frac{i}{4}(\mathcal{K}-\mathcal{H}),\quad\Phi_{a}^{1}\equiv-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{B}_{a},\quad\Phi^{4}\equiv\frac{i}{4}(\mathcal{H}+\mathcal{K}).

(B.62)

Therefore, the
Q
-transformation of
\mathcal{Z}
can be written as

\displaystyle\delta_{Q}(\epsilon)\mathcal{Z}=\frac{1}{2}(i\mathcal{H}-\gamma^{a}\mathcal{B}_{a}-i\gamma_{*}\gamma^{a}\mathcal{D}_{a}\mathcal{C})\epsilon,

(B.63)

\displaystyle\delta_{Q}(\epsilon)P_{L}\mathcal{Z}=\frac{1}{2}P_{L}(i\mathcal{H}-\gamma^{a}\mathcal{B}_{a}-i\gamma^{a}\mathcal{D}_{a}\mathcal{C})\epsilon,

(B.64)

\displaystyle\delta_{Q}(\epsilon)P_{R}\mathcal{Z}=\frac{1}{2}P_{R}(-i\mathcal{K}-\gamma^{a}\mathcal{B}_{a}+i\gamma^{a}\mathcal{D}_{a}\mathcal{C})\epsilon.

(B.65)

Following this procedure, it is possible to identify all the components of the superconformal multiplet
\mathcal{V}
and its complex conjugate multiplet
\mathcal{V}^{*}
, which are usually represented by the following collection

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{(w,c)}\equiv\Big(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K},\mathcal{B}_{a},\Lambda,\mathcal{D}\Big),

(B.66)

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{(w,-c)}^{*}\equiv\Big(\mathcal{C}^{*},\mathcal{Z}^{C},\mathcal{K}^{*},{\mathcal{H}^{*}},\mathcal{B}_{a},\Lambda^{C},\mathcal{D}^{*}\Big),

(B.67)

where
C
denotes the charge conjugation on the spinor; for the Majorana spinor represention, it becomes the complex conjugate, and
\mathcal{V}_{(w,n)}
means its lowest component has the Weyl/chiral weights (
w,c
). The transformation laws of all the components of the superconformal multiplet
\mathcal{V}_{(w,c)}
can be found follows:

•

\mathcal{C}~(w,c)
:

\displaystyle\delta_{Q}\mathcal{C}=\frac{i}{2}\bar{\epsilon}\gamma_{*}\mathcal{Z},\quad\delta_{M}\mathcal{C}=0,\quad\delta_{D}\mathcal{C}=w\lambda_{D}\mathcal{C},\quad\delta_{A}\mathcal{C}=in\theta\mathcal{C},\quad\delta_{S}\mathcal{C}=0,\quad\delta_{K}\mathcal{C}=0.

(B.68)

•

P_{L}\mathcal{Z}~(w+1/2,c-3/2)
,
P_{R}\mathcal{Z}~(w+1/2,c+3/2)
:

\displaystyle\delta_{Q}(\epsilon)P_{L}\mathcal{Z}=\frac{1}{2}P_{L}(i\mathcal{H}-\gamma^{a}\mathcal{B}_{a}-i\gamma^{a}\mathcal{D}_{a}\mathcal{C})\epsilon,\quad\delta_{M}P_{L}\mathcal{Z}=-\frac{1}{4}\lambda^{ab}\gamma_{ab}P_{L}\mathcal{Z},

\displaystyle\delta_{D}P_{L}\mathcal{Z}=(w+1/2)\lambda_{D}P_{L}\mathcal{Z},\quad\delta_{A}P_{L}\mathcal{Z}=i(c-3/2)\theta P_{L}\mathcal{Z},

\displaystyle\delta_{S}P_{L}\mathcal{Z}=-i(w+c)P_{L}\eta\mathcal{C},\quad\delta_{K}P_{L}\mathcal{Z}=0.,

(B.69)

\displaystyle\delta_{Q}(\epsilon)P_{R}\mathcal{Z}=\frac{1}{2}P_{R}(-i\mathcal{K}-\gamma^{a}\mathcal{B}_{a}+i\gamma^{a}\mathcal{D}_{a}\mathcal{C})\epsilon,\quad\delta_{M}P_{R}\mathcal{Z}=-\frac{1}{4}\lambda^{ab}\gamma_{ab}P_{R}\mathcal{Z},

\displaystyle\delta_{D}P_{R}\mathcal{Z}=(w+1/2)\lambda_{D}P_{R}\mathcal{Z},\quad\delta_{A}P_{R}\mathcal{Z}=i(c+3/2)\theta P_{R}\mathcal{Z},

\displaystyle\delta_{S}P_{R}\mathcal{Z}=i(w-c)P_{R}\eta\mathcal{C},\quad\delta_{K}P_{R}\mathcal{Z}=0.\qquad

(B.70)

•

\mathcal{H}~(w+1,c-3)
:

\displaystyle\delta_{Q}\mathcal{H}=-i\bar{\epsilon}P_{R}(\gamma^{a}\mathcal{D}_{a}\mathcal{Z}+\Lambda),\quad\delta_{M}\mathcal{H}=0,\quad\delta_{D}\mathcal{H}=(w+1)\lambda_{D}\mathcal{H},\quad\delta_{A}\mathcal{H}=i\theta(c-3)\mathcal{H},

\displaystyle\delta_{S}\mathcal{H}=i\bar{\eta}(w-2+c)P_{L}\mathcal{Z},\quad\delta_{K}\mathcal{H}=0.

(B.71)

•

\mathcal{K}~(w+1,c+3)
:

\displaystyle\delta_{Q}\mathcal{K}=i\bar{\epsilon}P_{L}(\gamma^{a}\mathcal{D}_{a}\mathcal{Z}+\Lambda),\quad\delta_{M}\mathcal{K}=0,\quad\delta_{D}\mathcal{K}=(w+1)\lambda_{D}\mathcal{K},\quad\delta_{A}\mathcal{K}=i\theta(c+3)\mathcal{K},

\displaystyle\delta_{S}\mathcal{K}=i\bar{\eta}(-w+2+c)P_{R}\mathcal{Z},\quad\delta_{K}\mathcal{K}=0.

(B.72)

•

\mathcal{B}_{a}~(w+1,c)
:

\displaystyle\delta_{Q}\mathcal{B}_{a}=-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\epsilon}(\gamma_{a}\Lambda+\mathcal{D}_{a}\mathcal{Z}),\quad\delta_{M}\mathcal{B}_{a}=-\lambda_{a}^{~~b}\mathcal{B}_{b},\quad\delta_{D}\mathcal{B}_{a}=(w+1)\lambda_{D}\mathcal{B}_{a},\quad\delta_{A}\mathcal{B}_{a}=ic\theta\mathcal{B}_{a},

\displaystyle\delta_{S}\mathcal{B}_{a}=\frac{1}{2}\bar{\eta}[(w+1)+c\gamma_{*}]\gamma_{a}\mathcal{Z},\quad\delta_{K}\mathcal{B}_{a}=-2i\lambda_{K,a}c\mathcal{C}

(B.73)

•

P_{L}\Lambda~(w+3/2,c-3/2)
,
P_{R}\Lambda~(w+3/2,c+3/2)
:

\displaystyle\delta_{Q}P_{L}\Lambda=\frac{1}{2}[\gamma^{ab}(\mathcal{D}_{a}\mathcal{B}_{b}-i\mathcal{D}_{a}\mathcal{D}_{b}\mathcal{C})+i\mathcal{D}]P_{L}\epsilon,\quad\delta_{M}P_{L}\Lambda=-\frac{1}{4}\lambda^{ab}\gamma_{ab}P_{L}\Lambda,

\displaystyle\delta_{D}P_{L}\Lambda=(w+3/2)\lambda_{D}P_{L}\Lambda,\quad\delta_{A}P_{L}\Lambda=i\theta(c+3/2)P_{L}\Lambda,

\displaystyle\delta_{S}P_{L}\Lambda=-\frac{1}{2}P_{L}(i\mathcal{K}+\gamma^{a}\mathcal{B}_{a}+i\gamma^{a}\mathcal{D}_{a}\mathcal{C})(w+c\gamma_{*})\eta,\quad\delta_{K}P_{L}\Lambda=\lambda_{K}^{a}(w+c)\gamma_{a}P_{R}\mathcal{Z},

(B.74)

\displaystyle\delta_{Q}P_{R}\Lambda=\frac{1}{2}[\gamma^{ab}(\mathcal{D}_{a}\mathcal{B}_{b}+i\mathcal{D}_{a}\mathcal{D}_{b}\mathcal{C})-i\mathcal{D}]P_{R}\epsilon,\quad\delta_{M}P_{R}\Lambda=-\frac{1}{4}\lambda^{ab}\gamma_{ab}P_{R}\Lambda,

\displaystyle\delta_{D}P_{R}\Lambda=(w-3/2)\lambda_{D}P_{R}\Lambda,\quad\delta_{A}P_{R}\Lambda=i\theta(c-3/2)P_{R}\Lambda,

\displaystyle\delta_{S}P_{R}\Lambda=\frac{1}{2}P_{R}(i\mathcal{H}-\gamma^{a}\mathcal{B}_{a}+i\gamma^{a}\mathcal{D}_{a}\mathcal{C})(w+c\gamma_{*})\eta,\quad\delta_{K}P_{R}\Lambda=\lambda_{K}^{a}(w-c)\gamma_{a}P_{L}\mathcal{Z}

(B.75)

•

\mathcal{D}~(w+2,c)
:

\displaystyle\delta_{Q}\mathcal{D}=\frac{i}{2}\bar{\epsilon}\gamma_{*}\gamma^{a}\mathcal{D}_{a}\Lambda,\quad\delta_{M}\mathcal{D}=0,\quad\delta_{D}\mathcal{D}=(w+2)\lambda_{D}\mathcal{D},\quad\delta_{A}\mathcal{D}=ic\theta\mathcal{D},

\displaystyle\delta_{S}\mathcal{D}=i\bar{\eta}(w\gamma_{*}+c)(\frac{1}{2}\gamma^{a}\mathcal{D}_{a}\mathcal{Z}+\Lambda),\quad\delta_{K}\mathcal{D}=2\lambda_{K}^{a}(w\mathcal{D}_{a}\mathcal{C}+ic\mathcal{B}_{a}).

(B.76)

If there are internal symmetries
\delta_{G}\mathcal{C}^{I}=\theta^{A}k_{A}^{I}(\mathcal{C}^{I})
of the lowest components
\mathcal{C}^{I}
from several superconformal multiplets
\mathcal{V}^{I}
labeled by an index
I
, then we have to add additional contributions to the above transformations, which are given by

\displaystyle\delta_{Q}\mathcal{B}_{a}^{I}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2}\bar{\epsilon}\gamma_{a}\gamma_{*}(\Lambda^{G})^{A}k_{A}^{I}(\mathcal{C}),

(B.77)

\displaystyle\delta_{Q}\Lambda^{I}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg[-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{D}^{A}_{G}k_{A}^{I}(\mathcal{C})+\frac{1}{4}\{(\bar{\Lambda}^{G})^{A}\gamma^{a}\mathcal{Z}^{J}\}\partial_{J}k_{A}^{I}\gamma_{a}+\frac{1}{4}\{(\bar{\Lambda}^{G})^{A}\gamma_{*}\gamma_{a}\mathcal{Z}^{J}\}\partial_{J}k_{A}^{I}\gamma_{*}\gamma_{a}\bigg]\epsilon,

(B.78)

\displaystyle\delta_{Q}\mathcal{D}^{I}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\bar{\epsilon}\mathcal{Z}^{J}\partial_{J}k_{A}^{I}(\mathcal{C})\mathcal{D}_{G}^{A}+\frac{i}{2}\bar{\epsilon}\gamma_{*}\gamma^{a}\mathcal{B}_{a}^{I}(\Lambda^{G})^{A}\partial_{J}k_{A}^{I}(\mathcal{C})-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\epsilon}\gamma^{a}\mathcal{D}_{a}(k_{A}^{I}(\mathcal{C})(\Lambda^{G})^{A}),

(B.79)

where
(\Lambda^{G})^{A},\mathcal{D}_{G}^{A}
are from the components of gauge multiplets
\mathcal{V}_{G}
.

The
superconformal covariant derivatives
of the fields of a superconformal multiplet
\mathcal{V}
under the superconformal symmetry are as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\mu}\mathcal{C}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\partial_{\mu}-wb_{\mu}-icA_{\mu})\mathcal{C}-\frac{i}{2}\bar{\psi}_{\mu}\gamma_{*}\mathcal{Z},

(B.80)

\displaystyle P_{L}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}\mathcal{Z}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\partial_{\mu}-(w+1/2)b_{\mu}-i(c-3/2)A_{\mu}+\frac{1}{4}\omega_{\mu}^{ab}\gamma_{ab}\Big)\mathcal{Z}

(B.81)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}P_{L}(i\mathcal{H}-\gamma^{a}\mathcal{B}_{a}-i\gamma^{a}\mathcal{D}_{a}\mathcal{C})\psi_{\mu}-i(w+c)P_{L}\phi_{\mu}\mathcal{C},

\displaystyle P_{R}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}\mathcal{Z}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\partial_{\mu}-(w+1/2)b_{\mu}-i(c+3/2)A_{\mu}+\frac{1}{4}\omega_{\mu}^{ab}\gamma_{ab}\Big)\mathcal{Z}

(B.82)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}P_{R}(-i\mathcal{K}-\gamma^{a}\mathcal{B}_{a}+i\gamma^{a}\mathcal{D}_{a}\mathcal{C})\psi_{\mu}-i(w-c)P_{R}\phi_{\mu}\mathcal{C},

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{a}\mathcal{B}_{b}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{\mu}_{a}\Big[(\partial_{\mu}-(w+1)b_{\mu}-icA_{\mu})\mathcal{B}_{b}+\omega_{\mu bc}\mathcal{B}^{c}

(B.83)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\bar{\psi}_{\mu}(\mathcal{D}_{b}\mathcal{Z}+\gamma_{b}\Lambda)-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\phi}_{\mu}(w+1+c\gamma_{*})\gamma_{b}\mathcal{Z}+2ic\mathcal{C}f_{\mu b}\Big],

\displaystyle P_{L}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}\Lambda

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{L}\Big(\partial_{\mu}-(w+3/2)b_{\mu}-i(c+3/2)A_{\mu}+\frac{1}{4}\omega_{\mu}^{ab}\gamma_{ab}\Big)\Lambda

(B.84)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}[\gamma^{ab}(\mathcal{D}_{a}\mathcal{B}_{b}-i\mathcal{D}_{a}\mathcal{D}_{b}\mathcal{C})+i\mathcal{D}]P_{L}\psi_{\mu}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}P_{L}(i\mathcal{K}+\gamma^{a}\mathcal{B}_{a}+i\gamma^{a}\mathcal{D}_{a}\mathcal{C})(w+c\gamma_{*})\phi_{\mu}-(w+c)\gamma_{a}P_{R}\mathcal{Z}f_{\mu}^{a},

\displaystyle P_{R}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}\Lambda

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{R}\Big(\partial_{\mu}-(w+3/2)b_{\mu}-i(c-3/2)A_{\mu}+\frac{1}{4}\omega_{\mu}^{ab}\gamma_{ab}\Big)\Lambda

(B.85)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}[\gamma^{ab}(\mathcal{D}_{a}\mathcal{B}_{b}+i\mathcal{D}_{a}\mathcal{D}_{b}\mathcal{C})-i\mathcal{D}]P_{R}\psi_{\mu}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}P_{R}(i\mathcal{H}-\gamma^{a}\mathcal{B}_{a}+i\gamma^{a}\mathcal{D}_{a}\mathcal{C})(w+c\gamma_{*})\phi_{\mu}-(w-c)\gamma_{a}P_{L}\mathcal{Z}f_{\mu}^{a},

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{[a}\mathcal{D}_{b]}\mathcal{C}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}[wR_{ab}(D)+icR_{ab}(A)]\mathcal{C}-\frac{i}{4}\overline{R_{ab}(Q)}\gamma_{*}\mathcal{Z}.

(B.86)

B.4.2
Chiral superconformal multiplet

A chiral supermultiplet is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}=\{X,P_{L}\Omega,F\}.

(B.87)

In the superconformal formalism, this can be represented by imposing chiral condition
P_{R}\mathcal{Z}\equiv 0
, so that the other consistency conditions are followed as

\displaystyle P_{R}\mathcal{Z}\equiv 0\implies\mathcal{K}=\Lambda=\mathcal{D}=0,\quad\mathcal{B}_{\mu}=i\mathcal{D}_{\mu}\mathcal{C},

(B.88)

which gives the following collection as a
chiral superconformal multiplet

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}=\Big(X,-i\sqrt{2}P_{L}\Omega,-2F,0,i\mathcal{D}_{\mu}X,0,0\Big).

(B.89)

The complex conjugate of this superconformal multiplet can be obtained by imposing the chiral condition
P_{L}\mathcal{Z}=0
, leading to

\displaystyle P_{L}\mathcal{Z}\equiv 0\implies\mathcal{H}=\Lambda=\mathcal{D}=0,\quad\mathcal{B}_{\mu}=-i\mathcal{D}_{\mu}\mathcal{C}^{*},

(B.90)

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}^{*}=\Big(X^{*},i\sqrt{2}P_{R}\Omega,0,-2F^{*},-i\mathcal{D}_{\mu}X^{*},0,0\Big).

(B.91)

In fact, the chiral superconformal multiplet
\mathcal{X}
can only be obtained when Weyl weight is equal to chiral weight, i.e.
w=c
. The reason for this is that when we impose the chiral confition
P_{R}\mathcal{Z}=0
, its
S
-transformation must vanish as well, resulting in

\displaystyle\delta_{S}P_{R}\mathcal{Z}=i(w-c)P_{R}\eta\mathcal{C}\overset{!}{=}0\implies w=c.

(B.92)

Meanwhile, there is another way of obtaining a chiral superconformal multiplet from the genral one. Let us take a look at the superconformal transformations of
\mathcal{K}
. If we impose
c=w-2
, the transformation laws of a scalar
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\equiv-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{K}
are given by

\displaystyle\delta_{Q}\mathcal{C}^{\prime}=-\frac{1}{2}i\bar{\epsilon}P_{L}(\gamma^{a}\mathcal{D}_{a}\mathcal{Z}+\Lambda),\quad\delta_{M}\mathcal{C}^{\prime}=0,\quad\delta_{D}\mathcal{C}^{\prime}=(w+1)\lambda_{D}\mathcal{C}^{\prime},

\displaystyle\delta_{A}\mathcal{C}^{\prime}=i\theta(w+1)\mathcal{C}^{\prime},\quad\delta_{S}\mathcal{C}^{\prime}=0,\quad\delta_{K}\mathcal{C}^{\prime}=0.

(B.93)

We notice that these transformations are those of a chiral superconformal multiplet whose lowest component is given by
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}=-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{K}
and the chiral fermion is identified with
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}=-P_{L}(\gamma^{a}\mathcal{D}_{a}\mathcal{Z}+\Lambda)
. Therefore, we can define an operation called
Chiral projection

T
such that

\displaystyle T(\mathcal{C}_{(w,c=w-2)})_{(w+1,w+1)}\equiv\mathcal{X}^{\prime}=\Big(-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{K},-\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}P_{L}(\gamma^{a}\mathcal{D}_{a}\mathcal{Z}+\Lambda),\frac{1}{2}(\mathcal{D}+\square^{C}\mathcal{C}+i\mathcal{D}^{a}\mathcal{B}_{a})\Big),

(B.94)

\displaystyle T(\mathcal{C}^{*}_{(w,c=w-2)})_{(w+1,w+1)}\equiv\mathcal{X}^{\prime}=\Big(-\frac{1}{2}{\mathcal{H}^{*}},\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}P_{R}(\gamma^{a}\mathcal{D}_{a}\mathcal{Z}^{C}+\Lambda^{C}),\frac{1}{2}(\mathcal{D}^{*}+\square^{C}\mathcal{C}^{*}-i\mathcal{D}^{a}\mathcal{B}_{a}^{*})\Big),

Note that the Weyl/chiral weights changes from
(w,c=w-2)
to
(w+1,c=w+1)
. Hence, we can say that
T
carries the Weyl/chiral weights
(1,3)
!

Plus, there is a special case that the chiral projection operation
T(\cdot)
can be applied to a chiral multiplet with
w=1
, i.e.
\mathcal{X}_{(1,1)}\equiv\{X,P_{L}\Omega,F\}
. Then, the corresponding chiral projection is

\displaystyle T(\mathcal{X}_{(1,1)})_{(2,2)}=\Big(F^{*},\quad\gamma^{a}\mathcal{D}_{a}P_{R}\Omega,\quad\square^{C}X^{*}\Big).

(B.96)

B.4.3
Real superconformal multiplet

It is also possible to obtain a real superconformal multiplet by imposing a real condition that

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}^{*}=\mathcal{C}\equiv C,\quad(P_{L}\mathcal{Z})^{C}=P_{R}\mathcal{Z}\equiv P_{R}\zeta,\quad(P_{R}\mathcal{Z})^{C}=P_{L}\mathcal{Z}\equiv P_{L}\zeta,\quad{\mathcal{H}^{*}}=\mathcal{K},

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{a}^{*}=\mathcal{B}_{a}\equiv B_{a},\quad(P_{L}\Lambda)^{C}=P_{R}\Lambda\equiv P_{R}\lambda,\quad(P_{R}\Lambda)^{C}=P_{L}\Lambda\equiv P_{L}\lambda,\quad\mathcal{D}^{*}=\mathcal{D}\equiv D.

(B.97)

The real multiplt
V
is then given by

\displaystyle V=\Big(C,\zeta,\mathcal{H},{\mathcal{H}^{*}},B_{a},\lambda,D\Big).

(B.98)

B.4.4
Linear superconformal multiplet

Using the chiral projection operation, we can define a complex linear superconformal multiplet
\mathcal{V}_{L}
by imposing two conditions
c=w-2
and

\displaystyle T(\mathcal{V}_{L})\equiv 0.

(B.99)

In particular, by requiring reality to
L
in addition to the two conditions, we get a real linear superconformal multiplet given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{L}=\Big(C^{L},\zeta^{L},0,0,B_{a}^{L},-\gamma^{a}\mathcal{D}_{a}\zeta^{L},-\square^{C}C^{L}\Big),

(B.100)

where
C^{L}
is a real scalar,
\zeta^{L}
is a Majorana spinor, and
B_{a}^{L}
is a real vector such that
\mathcal{D}^{a}B_{a}^{L}=0
.

B.4.5
Gauge superconformal multiplet

Another crucial multiplet is a
Gauge superconformal multiplet
. To get this, we can start with a real superconformal multiplet with the Weyl/chiral weight (0,0):

\displaystyle V^{A}_{G}=\Big(C^{A},\zeta^{A},\mathcal{H}^{A},{\mathcal{H}^{*}}^{A},B_{a}^{A},(\lambda^{G})^{A},(D^{G})^{A}\Big).

(B.101)

Then, let us look at the following field redefinition

\displaystyle\hat{B}_{\mu}\equiv e^{a}_{\mu}B_{a}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\psi}_{\mu}\zeta.

(B.102)

Then, the
Q
-transformation of this vector field is given by

\displaystyle\delta_{Q}\hat{B}_{\mu}=-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\epsilon}\gamma_{\mu}\lambda-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\mu}(\bar{\epsilon}\zeta).

(B.103)

Here we see that the last term may be thought as a
U(1)
gauge transformation of a gauge field
\hat{B}_{\mu}\longrightarrow\hat{B}_{\mu}-\partial_{\mu}\theta
where
\theta
is a real scalar gauge parameter. We then establish a superconformal multiplet defined by

\displaystyle V^{A}_{G}=\Big(0,0,0,0,(\hat{B}_{a})^{A},(\lambda^{G})^{A},(D^{G})^{A}\Big),

(B.104)

where
(\hat{B}_{a})^{A}
is
gauge (vector) field
;
(\lambda^{G})^{A}
is its fermionic superpartner called
gaugino
, and
(D^{G})^{A}
is
auxiliary real scalar
of the gauge multiplet. Their transformations are specified as follows:

•

(\hat{B}_{\mu})^{A}
(
1,0
):

\displaystyle\delta_{Q}(\hat{B}_{\mu})^{A}=-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\epsilon}\gamma_{\mu}(\lambda^{G})^{A},\quad\delta_{M}(\hat{B}_{\mu})^{A}=\delta_{D}(\hat{B}_{\mu})^{A}=\delta_{A}(\hat{B}_{\mu})^{A}=\delta_{S}(\hat{B}_{\mu})^{A}=\delta_{K}(\hat{B}_{\mu})^{A}=0

(B.105)

Here notice that gauge field
(\hat{B}_{\mu})^{A}
transforms only under the Q-SUSY transformation, while it is inert under the others.

•

(\lambda^{G})^{A}
(
3/2,\pm 3/2
):

\displaystyle\delta_{Q}(\lambda^{G})^{A}=\Big(\frac{i}{2}\gamma_{*}(D^{G})^{A}+\frac{1}{4}\gamma^{ab}(\hat{F}^{G}_{ab})^{A}\Big)\epsilon,\quad\delta_{M}(\lambda^{G})^{A}=-\frac{1}{4}\lambda^{ab}\gamma_{ab}(\lambda^{G})^{A},

\displaystyle\delta_{D}(\lambda^{G})^{A}=\frac{3}{2}\lambda_{D}(\lambda^{G})^{A},\quad\delta_{A}(\lambda^{G})^{A}=\frac{3}{2}i\theta\gamma_{*}(\lambda^{G})^{A},\quad\delta_{S}(\lambda^{G})^{A}=\delta_{K}(\lambda^{G})^{A}=0,

\displaystyle\textrm{where~~}(\hat{F}_{ab}^{G})^{A}\equiv e^{\mu}_{a}e^{\nu}_{b}\Big(2\partial_{[\mu}(\hat{B}_{\nu]})^{A}+f_{BC}^{~~~A}(\hat{B}_{\mu})^{B}(\hat{B}_{\nu})^{C}+\bar{\psi}_{[\mu}\gamma_{\nu]}(\lambda^{G})^{A}\Big).

(B.106)

•

(D^{G})^{A}
(
2,0
):

\displaystyle\delta_{Q}(D^{G})^{A}=\frac{i}{2}\bar{\epsilon}\gamma_{*}(\gamma^{a}\mathcal{D}_{a}\lambda^{G})^{A},\quad\delta_{M}(D^{G})^{A}=0,\quad\delta_{D}(D^{G})^{A}=2\lambda_{D}(D^{G})^{A},

\displaystyle\delta_{s}(D^{G})^{A}=\delta_{K}(D^{G})^{A}=0.

(B.107)

It is obvious that the Q-SUSY transformations of a general superconformal multiplet will change due to the additional shifts caused by internal gauge symmetries. When a multiplet is charged under gauge symmetries, a partial derivative must be replaced by a proper covariant derivative which involves the gauge-coupling part given by
\partial_{\mu}\longrightarrow D_{\mu}\supset-(\hat{B}_{\mu})^{A}k_{A}^{I}(\mathcal{C})
. Since the gauge field transforms under Q-SUSY, a transformation of the covariant derivative of some fields of the gauged multiplet must depend on the gaugino, which is completely new compared to the Q-SUSY transformations of the un-gauged case. The additional corrections to the Q-SUSY transformations are shown in Eq. (
B.79
). In addition, a gauged chiral superconformal multiplet is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{gauged}=\Big(X,-i\sqrt{2}P_{L}\Omega,-2F,-2iF,i\mathcal{D}_{a}X,-2iP_{R}(\lambda^{G})^{A}k_{A}(X),-ik_{A}(X)(D^{G})^{A}\Big),

(B.108)

where
k_{A}(X)
is the Killing vector of gauge symmetries.

B.5
Multiplication Laws of Superconformal multiplets

In this section, I introduce the multiplication laws of the superconformal multiplets from Ref.
[
70
]
. Multiplication laws for a composite multiplet whose arguments are given only by
complex
superconformal multiplets:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{C}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f(\mathcal{C}^{i}),

(B.109)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f_{i}\mathcal{Z}^{i},

(B.110)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f_{i}\mathcal{H}^{i}-\frac{1}{2}f_{ij}\bar{\mathcal{Z}}^{i}P_{L}\mathcal{Z}^{j},

(B.111)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{K}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f_{i}\mathcal{K}^{i}-\frac{1}{2}f_{ij}\bar{\mathcal{Z}}^{i}P_{R}\mathcal{Z}^{j},

(B.112)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f_{i}\mathcal{B}_{\mu}^{i}+\frac{i}{4}f_{ij}\bar{\mathcal{Z}}^{i}\gamma_{*}\gamma_{\mu}\mathcal{Z}^{j}=f_{i}\mathcal{B}_{\mu}^{i}+\frac{i}{2}f_{ij}\bar{\mathcal{Z}}^{i}P_{L}\gamma_{\mu}\mathcal{Z}^{j},

(B.113)

\displaystyle\tilde{\Lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f_{i}\lambda^{i}+\frac{1}{2}f_{ij}\Big[i\gamma_{*}\cancel{\mathcal{B}}^{i}+P_{L}\mathcal{K}^{i}+P_{R}\mathcal{H}^{i}-\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\mathcal{C}^{i}\Big]\mathcal{Z}^{j}-\frac{1}{4}f_{ijk}\mathcal{Z}^{i}\bar{\mathcal{Z}}^{j}\mathcal{Z}^{k},

(B.114)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{D}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f_{i}\mathcal{D}^{i}+\frac{1}{2}f_{ij}\Big(\mathcal{K}^{i}\mathcal{H}^{j}-\mathcal{B}^{i}\cdot\mathcal{B}^{j}-\mathcal{D}\mathcal{C}^{i}\cdot\mathcal{D}\mathcal{C}^{j}-2\bar{\Lambda}\mathcal{Z}^{j}-\bar{\mathcal{Z}}^{i}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\mathcal{Z}^{j}\Big)

(B.115)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}f_{ijk}\bar{\mathcal{Z}}^{i}\Big(i\gamma_{*}\cancel{\mathcal{B}}^{i}+P_{L}\mathcal{K}^{i}+P_{R}\mathcal{H}^{i}\Big)\mathcal{Z}^{k}+\frac{1}{8}f_{ijkl}(\bar{\mathcal{Z}}^{i}P_{L}\mathcal{Z}^{j})(\bar{\mathcal{Z}}^{k}P_{R}\mathcal{Z}^{l}).

Multiplication laws fora composite multiplet whose arguments are given only by
chiral
superconformal multiplets:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{C}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f(X^{\alpha},\bar{X}^{\bar{\alpha}}),

(B.116)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\sqrt{2}(-f_{\alpha}\Omega^{\alpha}+f_{\bar{\alpha}}\Omega^{\bar{\alpha}}),

(B.117)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2f_{\alpha}F^{\alpha}+f_{\alpha\beta}\bar{\Omega}^{\alpha}\Omega^{\beta},

(B.118)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{K}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2f_{\bar{\alpha}}F^{\bar{\alpha}}+f_{\bar{\alpha}\bar{\beta}}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{\alpha}}\Omega^{\bar{\beta}},

(B.119)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle if_{\alpha}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}X^{\alpha}-if_{\bar{\alpha}}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}\bar{X}^{\bar{\alpha}}+if_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}\bar{\Omega}^{\alpha}\gamma_{\mu}\Omega^{\bar{\beta}},

(B.120)

\displaystyle P_{L}\tilde{\Lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{2}if_{\bar{\alpha}\beta}[(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}X^{\beta})\Omega^{\bar{\alpha}}-F^{\bar{\alpha}}\Omega^{\beta}]-\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}f_{\bar{\alpha}\bar{\beta}\gamma}\Omega^{\gamma}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{\alpha}}\Omega^{\bar{\beta}},

(B.121)

\displaystyle P_{R}\tilde{\Lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}if_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}[(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{X}^{\bar{\beta}})\Omega^{\alpha}-F^{\alpha}\Omega^{\bar{\beta}}]+\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}f_{\alpha\beta\bar{\gamma}}\Omega^{\bar{\gamma}}\bar{\Omega}^{\alpha}\Omega^{\beta},

(B.122)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{D}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2f_{\alpha\bar{\beta}}\Big(-\mathcal{D}_{\mu}X^{\alpha}\mathcal{D}^{\mu}\bar{X}^{\bar{\beta}}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Omega}^{\alpha}P_{L}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\Omega^{\bar{\beta}}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{\beta}}P_{R}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\Omega^{\alpha}+F^{\alpha}F^{\bar{\beta}}\Big)

(B.123)

\displaystyle+f_{\alpha\beta\bar{\gamma}}(-\bar{\Omega}^{\alpha}\Omega^{\beta}F^{\bar{\gamma}}+\bar{\Omega}^{\alpha}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}X^{\beta})\Omega^{\bar{\gamma}})+f_{\bar{\alpha}\bar{\beta}\gamma}(-\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{\alpha}}\Omega^{\bar{\beta}}F^{\gamma}+\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{\alpha}}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{X}^{\bar{\beta}})\Omega^{\gamma})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}f_{\alpha\beta\bar{\gamma}\bar{\delta}}(\bar{\Omega}^{\alpha}P_{L}\Omega^{\beta})(\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{\gamma}}P_{R}\Omega^{\bar{\delta}}).

Multiplication laws for a composite multiplet whose arguments are given only by
real
superconformal multiplets:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{C}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f(C^{i}),

(B.124)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f_{i}\zeta^{i},

(B.125)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f_{i}\mathcal{H}^{i}-\frac{1}{2}f_{ij}\bar{\zeta}^{i}P_{L}\zeta^{j},

(B.126)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{K}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f_{i}{\mathcal{H}^{*}}^{i}-\frac{1}{2}f_{ij}\bar{\zeta}^{i}P_{R}\zeta^{j},

(B.127)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f_{i}B_{\mu}^{i}+\frac{i}{4}f_{ij}\bar{\zeta}^{i}\gamma_{*}\gamma_{\mu}\zeta^{j},

(B.128)

\displaystyle\tilde{\Lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f_{i}\lambda^{i}+\frac{1}{2}f_{ij}\Big[i\gamma_{*}\cancel{B}^{i}+\textrm{Re}\mathcal{H}^{i}-i\gamma_{*}\textrm{Im}\mathcal{H}^{i}-\cancel{\mathcal{D}}C^{i}\Big]\zeta^{j}-\frac{1}{4}f_{ijk}\zeta^{i}\bar{\zeta}^{j}\zeta^{k},

(B.129)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{D}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f_{i}D^{i}+\frac{1}{2}f_{ij}\Big(\mathcal{H}^{i}{\mathcal{H}^{*}}^{j}-B^{i}\cdot B^{j}-\mathcal{D}C^{i}\cdot\mathcal{D}C^{j}-2\bar{\lambda}^{i}\zeta^{j}-\bar{\zeta}^{i}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\zeta^{j}\Big)

(B.130)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}f_{ijk}\bar{\zeta}^{i}\Big(i\gamma_{*}\cancel{B}^{i}+\textrm{Re}\mathcal{H}^{i}-i\gamma_{*}\textrm{Im}\mathcal{H}^{i}\Big)\zeta^{k}+\frac{1}{8}f_{ijkl}(\bar{\zeta}^{i}P_{L}\zeta^{j})(\bar{\zeta}^{k}P_{R}\zeta^{l}).

B.6
Density Formulae

•

F-term Density Formula
: For any chiral superconformal multiplet
\mathcal{X}
with the Weyl/chiral weights
(3,3)
whose supermultiplet form is given by
\{X,P_{L}\Omega,F\}
, the corresponding invariant action “
[X]_{F}
” via F-term sector can be obtained by the following expression:

\displaystyle[X]_{F}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int d^{4}xe\bigg[F+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\bar{\psi}_{\mu}\gamma^{\mu}P_{L}\Omega+\frac{1}{2}X\bar{\psi}_{\mu}\gamma^{\mu\nu}P_{R}\psi_{\nu}\bigg]+h.c.

(B.131)

where
e\equiv\det(e^{a}_{\mu})
. This F-term density formula form is the same as that of the Kugo’s notation.

•

D-term Density Formula
: For any real superconformal multiplet
\mathcal{V}
with Weyl/chiral weights
(2,0)
whose lowest component is given by a real scalar
C
, we can always construct a chiral superconformal multiplet with the Weyl/chiral weights
(3,3)
by applying the chiral projection operation
T
to the real multiplet
\mathcal{V}
. This means that it is possible to compute the corresponding invariant action using the above F-term density formula. Here, we define the D-term density formula in terms of the chiral projection and F-term density formula as follows:

\displaystyle[\mathcal{C}]_{D}\equiv\frac{1}{2}[T(\mathcal{C})]_{F}.

(B.132)

Also, we have
[T(\mathcal{C})]_{F}=[T(\mathcal{C}^{*})]
.
After integrating by parts, we reach the following equivalent invariant action “
[C]_{D}
” that can also be calculated via D-term sector:

\displaystyle[C]_{D}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\frac{1}{2}}\int d^{4}xe\bigg[D+\mathcal{D}^{a}\mathcal{D}_{a}C+\frac{1}{2}\Big(i\bar{\psi}\cdot\gamma P_{R}(\lambda+\gamma^{a}\mathcal{D}_{a}\zeta)-\frac{1}{4}\bar{\psi}_{\mu}P_{L}\gamma^{\mu\nu}\psi_{\nu}\mathcal{H}+h.c.\Big)\bigg]

(B.133)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\frac{1}{2}}\int d^{4}xe\bigg[D-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\psi}\cdot\gamma i\gamma_{*}\lambda-\frac{1}{3}CR(\omega)+\frac{1}{6}\Big(C\bar{\psi}_{\mu}\gamma^{\mu\rho\sigma}-i\bar{\zeta}\gamma^{\rho\sigma}\gamma_{*}\Big)R^{\prime}_{\rho\sigma}(Q)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+\frac{1}{4}\varepsilon^{abcd}\bar{\psi}_{a}\gamma_{b}\psi_{c}(B_{d}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\psi}_{d}\zeta)\Bigg],

where
R^{\prime}_{\rho\sigma}(Q)\equiv 2(\partial_{[\mu}+\frac{1}{4}\omega_{[\mu}^{ab}\gamma_{ab}+\frac{1}{2}b_{[\mu}-\frac{3}{2}iA_{[\mu}\gamma_{*})\psi_{\nu]}
, and
R(\omega)\equiv R_{\mu\nu}^{cov}(M)g^{\mu\nu}
. It is worth noticing that there is a factor of 1/2 in this D-term density formula in our notation (which follows Ferrara’s one), while in the Kugo’s notation, there is no the factor of 1/2.

•

Theorems on the chiral projection
:
T(Z\mathcal{C})=ZT(\mathcal{C})
is satisfied if
Z
is a chiral multiplet and
\mathcal{C}
has the Weyl/chiral weights
(w,w-2)
. Plus, for any two chiral multiplets
\Lambda
with
(0,0)
and
Z
with
(1,1)
,
[(\Lambda+\Lambda^{*})ZZ^{*}]_{D}=\frac{1}{2}[T\Big((\Lambda+\Lambda^{*})ZZ^{*}\Big)]_{F}=[\Lambda ZT(Z^{*})]_{F}
can be satisfied.

Appendix C
Derivation of Fermion Masses in the Supergravity Model of Inflation in Ch.
11

We consider matter chiral multiplets
Z^{i}
, the chiral compensator
S_{0}
, a real multiplet
V
, and another real multiplet

(V)_{D}
, whose lowest component is the auxiliary D term of the real multiplet
V
. Their superconformal multiplets are given as follows:

\displaystyle V=\{0,0,0,0,A_{\mu},\lambda,D\}~\textrm{in the Wess-Zumino gauge,~i.e.}~v=\zeta=\mathcal{H}=0,

(C.1)

\displaystyle Z^{i}=(z^{i},-i\sqrt{2}P_{L}\chi^{i},-2F^{i},0,+i\mathcal{D}_{\mu}z^{i},0,0)=\{z^{i},P_{L}\chi^{i},F^{i}\},

(C.2)

\displaystyle\bar{Z}^{\bar{i}}=(\bar{z}^{\bar{i}},+i\sqrt{2}P_{R}\chi^{\bar{i}},0,-2\bar{F}^{\bar{i}},-i\mathcal{D}_{\mu}\bar{z}^{\bar{i}},0,0)=\{\bar{z}^{\bar{i}},P_{R}\chi^{\bar{i}},\bar{F}^{\bar{i}}\},

(C.3)

\displaystyle S_{0}=(s_{0},-i\sqrt{2}P_{L}\chi^{0},-2F_{0},0,+i\mathcal{D}_{\mu}s_{0},0,0)=\{s_{0},P_{L}\chi^{0},F_{0}\},

(C.4)

\displaystyle\bar{S}_{0}=(\bar{s}_{0},+i\sqrt{2}P_{R}\chi^{0},0,-2\bar{F}_{0},-i\mathcal{D}_{\mu}\bar{s}_{0},0,0)=\{\bar{s}_{0},P_{R}\chi^{0},\bar{F}_{0}\},

(C.5)

\displaystyle\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda=(\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda,-i\sqrt{2}P_{L}\Lambda,2D_{-}^{2},0,+i\mathcal{D}_{\mu}(\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda),0,0)=\{\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda,P_{L}\Lambda,-D_{-}^{2}\},

(C.6)

\displaystyle\bar{\lambda}P_{R}\lambda=(\bar{\lambda}P_{R}\lambda,+i\sqrt{2}P_{R}\Lambda,0,2D_{+}^{2},-i\mathcal{D}_{\mu}(\bar{\lambda}P_{R}\lambda),0,0)=\{\bar{\lambda}P_{R}\lambda,P_{R}\Lambda,-D_{+}^{2}\},

(C.7)

\displaystyle(V)_{D}=(D,\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\lambda,0,0,\mathcal{D}^{b}\hat{F}_{ab},-\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\lambda,-\square^{C}D),

(C.8)

where

\displaystyle P_{L}\Lambda\equiv\sqrt{2}P_{L}(-\frac{1}{2}\gamma\cdot\hat{F}+iD)\lambda,\qquad P_{R}\Lambda\equiv\sqrt{2}P_{R}(-\frac{1}{2}\gamma\cdot\hat{F}-iD)\lambda,

(C.9)

\displaystyle D_{-}^{2}\equiv D^{2}-\hat{F}^{-}\cdot\hat{F}^{-}-2\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\lambda,\qquad D_{+}^{2}\equiv D^{2}-\hat{F}^{+}\cdot\hat{F}^{+}-2\bar{\lambda}P_{R}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\lambda,

(C.10)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\mu}\lambda\equiv\bigg(\partial_{\mu}-\frac{3}{2}b_{\mu}+\frac{1}{4}w_{\mu}^{ab}\gamma_{ab}-\frac{3}{2}i\gamma_{*}\mathcal{A}_{\mu}\bigg)\lambda-\bigg(\frac{1}{4}\gamma^{ab}\hat{F}_{ab}+\frac{1}{2}i\gamma_{*}D\bigg)\psi_{\mu}

(C.11)

\displaystyle\hat{F}_{ab}\equiv F_{ab}+e_{a}^{~\mu}e_{b}^{~\nu}\bar{\psi}_{[\mu}\gamma_{\nu]}\lambda,\qquad F_{ab}\equiv e_{a}^{~\mu}e_{b}^{~\nu}(2\partial_{[\mu}A_{\nu]}),

(C.12)

\displaystyle\hat{F}^{\pm}_{\mu\nu}\equiv\frac{1}{2}(\hat{F}_{\mu\nu}\pm\tilde{\hat{F}}_{\mu\nu}),\qquad\tilde{\hat{F}}_{\mu\nu}\equiv-\frac{1}{2}i\epsilon_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\hat{F}^{\rho\sigma}.

(C.13)

Next, we show the components of the first superconformal
composite
complex multiplets
w^{\prime 2}
and
\bar{w}^{\prime 2}
with Weyl/chiral weights
(-1,3)
and
(-1,-3)
respectively. These composite multiplets are defined to be

\displaystyle w^{\prime 2}\equiv\frac{\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda}{(S_{0}\bar{S}_{0}e^{-K/3})^{2}}=\{\mathcal{C}_{w},\mathcal{Z}_{w},\mathcal{H}_{w},\mathcal{K}_{w},\mathcal{B}^{w}_{\mu},\Lambda_{w},\mathcal{D}_{w}\}

(C.14)

\displaystyle\bar{w}^{\prime 2}\equiv\frac{\bar{\lambda}P_{R}\lambda}{(S_{0}\bar{S}_{0}e^{-K/3})^{2}}=\{\mathcal{C}_{\bar{w}},\mathcal{Z}_{\bar{w}},\mathcal{H}_{\bar{w}},\mathcal{K}_{\bar{w}},\mathcal{B}^{\bar{w}}_{\mu},\Lambda_{\bar{w}},\mathcal{D}_{\bar{w}}\}.

(C.15)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{w}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h\equiv\frac{\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda}{(s_{0}\bar{s}_{0}e^{-K(z,\bar{z})/3})^{2}},

(C.16)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{w}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\sqrt{2}(-h_{a}\Omega^{a}+h_{\bar{a}}\Omega^{\bar{a}}),

(C.17)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{w}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2h_{a}F^{a}+h_{ab}\bar{\Omega}^{a}\Omega^{b},

(C.18)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{w}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2h_{\bar{a}}F^{\bar{a}}+h_{\bar{a}\bar{b}}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{a}}\Omega^{\bar{b}},

(C.19)

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{w}_{\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle ih_{a}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}X^{a}-ih_{\bar{a}}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}\bar{X}^{\bar{a}}+ih_{a\bar{b}}\bar{\Omega}^{a}\gamma_{\mu}\Omega^{\bar{b}},

(C.20)

\displaystyle P_{L}\Lambda_{w}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{2}ih_{\bar{a}b}[(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}X^{b})\Omega^{\bar{a}}-F^{\bar{a}}\Omega^{b}]-\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}h_{\bar{a}\bar{b}c}\Omega^{c}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{a}}\Omega^{\bar{b}},

(C.21)

\displaystyle P_{R}\Lambda_{w}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}ih_{a\bar{b}}[(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{X}^{\bar{b}})\Omega^{a}-F^{a}\Omega^{\bar{b}}]+\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}h_{ab\bar{c}}\Omega^{\bar{c}}\bar{\Omega}^{a}\Omega^{b},

(C.22)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{w}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2h_{a\bar{b}}\Big(-\mathcal{D}_{\mu}X^{a}\mathcal{D}^{\mu}\bar{X}^{\bar{b}}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Omega}^{a}P_{L}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\Omega^{\bar{b}}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{b}}P_{R}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\Omega^{a}+F^{a}F^{\bar{b}}\Big)

(C.23)

\displaystyle+h_{ab\bar{c}}(-\bar{\Omega}^{a}\Omega^{b}F^{\bar{c}}+\bar{\Omega}^{a}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}X^{b})\Omega^{\bar{c}})+h_{\bar{a}\bar{b}c}(-\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{a}}\Omega^{\bar{b}}F^{c}+\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{a}}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{X}^{\bar{b}})\Omega^{c})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}h_{ab\bar{c}\bar{d}}(\bar{\Omega}^{a}P_{L}\Omega^{b})(\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{c}}P_{R}\Omega^{\bar{d}}).

Notice that when finding the multiplet
\bar{w}^{\prime 2}
, we can just replace
h
by its complex conjugate
h^{*}
.

The second superconformal multiplets that we need are the
composite
chiral projection multiplets
T(\bar{w}^{\prime 2})
and
\bar{T}(w^{\prime 2})
with Weyl/chiral weights
(0,0)
. From their component supermultiplets defined by

\displaystyle T(\bar{w}^{\prime 2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{K}_{\bar{w}},-\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{2}iP_{L}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\mathcal{Z}_{\bar{w}}+\Lambda_{\bar{w}}),\frac{1}{2}(\mathcal{D}_{\bar{w}}+\square^{C}\mathcal{C}_{\bar{w}}+i\mathcal{D}_{a}\mathcal{B}^{a}_{\bar{w}})\right),

(C.24)

\displaystyle\bar{T}(w^{\prime 2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{K}_{\bar{w}}^{*},\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{2}iP_{R}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\mathcal{Z}_{\bar{w}}^{C}+\Lambda_{\bar{w}}^{C}),\frac{1}{2}(\mathcal{D}_{\bar{w}}^{*}+\square^{C}\mathcal{C}_{\bar{w}}^{*}-i\mathcal{D}_{a}(\mathcal{B}^{a}_{\bar{w}})^{*})\right)

(C.25)

we find the corresponding superconformal multiplets and their complex conjugates as follows:

\displaystyle T\equiv T(\bar{w}^{\prime 2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{\mathcal{C}_{T},\mathcal{Z}_{T},\mathcal{H}_{T},\mathcal{K}_{T},\mathcal{B}_{\mu}^{T},\Lambda_{T},\mathcal{D}_{T}\}

\displaystyle\bar{T}\equiv\bar{T}(w^{\prime 2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{\mathcal{C}_{\bar{T}},\mathcal{Z}_{\bar{T}},\mathcal{H}_{\bar{T}},\mathcal{K}_{\bar{T}},\mathcal{B}_{\mu}^{\bar{T}},\Lambda_{\bar{T}},\mathcal{D}_{\bar{T}}\},

(C.26)

whose superconformal components are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{K}_{\bar{w}}=h^{*}_{\bar{a}}F^{\bar{a}}-\frac{1}{2}h^{*}_{\bar{a}\bar{b}}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{a}}\Omega^{\bar{b}}\equiv C_{T}

(C.27)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{2}iP_{L}\bigg[\cancel{\mathcal{D}}(-h^{*}_{a}\Omega^{a}+h^{*}_{\bar{a}}\Omega^{\bar{a}})-h^{*}_{\bar{a}b}[(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}X^{b})\Omega^{\bar{a}}-F^{\bar{a}}\Omega^{b}]-\frac{1}{2}h^{*}_{\bar{a}\bar{b}c}\Omega^{c}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{a}}\Omega^{\bar{b}}

(C.28)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+h^{*}_{a\bar{b}}[(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{X}^{\bar{b}})\Omega^{a}-F^{a}\Omega^{\bar{b}}]+\frac{1}{2}h^{*}_{ab\bar{c}}\Omega^{\bar{c}}\bar{\Omega}^{a}\Omega^{b}\bigg]\equiv-\sqrt{2}iP_{L}\Omega_{T},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\bigg[h^{*}_{a\bar{b}}\Big(-\mathcal{D}_{\mu}X^{a}\mathcal{D}^{\mu}\bar{X}^{\bar{b}}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Omega}^{a}P_{L}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\Omega^{\bar{b}}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{b}}P_{R}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\Omega^{a}+F^{a}F^{\bar{b}}\Big)

(C.29)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}h^{*}_{ab\bar{c}}(-\bar{\Omega}^{a}\Omega^{b}F^{\bar{c}}+\bar{\Omega}^{a}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}X^{b})\Omega^{\bar{c}})+\frac{1}{2}h^{*}_{\bar{a}\bar{b}c}(-\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{a}}\Omega^{\bar{b}}F^{c}+\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{a}}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{X}^{\bar{b}})\Omega^{c})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}h^{*}_{ab\bar{c}\bar{d}}(\bar{\Omega}^{a}P_{L}\Omega^{b})(\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{c}}P_{R}\Omega^{\bar{d}})+\frac{1}{2}\square^{C}h^{*}+\frac{1}{2}i\mathcal{D}^{\mu}(ih^{*}_{a}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}X^{a}-ih^{*}_{\bar{a}}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}\bar{X}^{\bar{a}}+ih^{*}_{a\bar{b}}\bar{\Omega}^{a}\gamma_{\mu}\Omega^{\bar{b}})\bigg]

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle-2F_{T},

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0,

(C.30)

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{T}_{\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i\mathcal{D}_{\mu}\mathcal{C}_{T},

(C.31)

\displaystyle\Lambda_{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0,

(C.32)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0,

(C.33)

where we used
a,b,c,d=0,i(\equiv z^{i}),W(\equiv\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda)
. This gives the superfield components of the chiral projection multiplet
T
:

\displaystyle T(\bar{w}^{\prime 2})=(C_{T},P_{L}\Omega_{T},F_{T})

(C.34)

where

\displaystyle C_{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h^{*}_{\bar{a}}F^{\bar{a}}-\frac{1}{2}h^{*}_{\bar{a}\bar{b}}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{a}}\Omega^{\bar{b}},

(C.35)

\displaystyle P_{L}\Omega_{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\cancel{\mathcal{D}}(-h^{*}_{a}\Omega^{a}+h^{*}_{\bar{a}}\Omega^{\bar{a}})-h^{*}_{\bar{a}b}[(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}X^{b})\Omega^{\bar{a}}-F^{\bar{a}}\Omega^{b}]-\frac{1}{2}h^{*}_{\bar{a}\bar{b}c}\Omega^{c}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{a}}\Omega^{\bar{b}}

(C.36)

\displaystyle+h^{*}_{a\bar{b}}[(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{X}^{\bar{b}})\Omega^{a}-F^{a}\Omega^{\bar{b}}]+\frac{1}{2}h^{*}_{ab\bar{c}}\Omega^{\bar{c}}\bar{\Omega}^{a}\Omega^{b},

\displaystyle F_{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h^{*}_{a\bar{b}}\Big(-\mathcal{D}_{\mu}X^{a}\mathcal{D}^{\mu}\bar{X}^{\bar{b}}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Omega}^{a}P_{L}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\Omega^{\bar{b}}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{b}}P_{R}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\Omega^{a}+F^{a}F^{\bar{b}}\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}h^{*}_{ab\bar{c}}(-\bar{\Omega}^{a}\Omega^{b}F^{\bar{c}}+\bar{\Omega}^{a}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}X^{b})\Omega^{\bar{c}})+\frac{1}{2}h^{*}_{\bar{a}\bar{b}c}(-\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{a}}\Omega^{\bar{b}}F^{c}+\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{a}}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{X}^{\bar{b}})\Omega^{c})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}h^{*}_{ab\bar{c}\bar{d}}(\bar{\Omega}^{a}P_{L}\Omega^{b})(\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{c}}P_{R}\Omega^{\bar{d}})+\frac{1}{2}\square^{C}h^{*}-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{D}^{\mu}(h^{*}_{a}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}X^{a}-h^{*}_{\bar{a}}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}\bar{X}^{\bar{a}}+h^{*}_{a\bar{b}}\bar{\Omega}^{a}\gamma_{\mu}\Omega^{\bar{b}}).

Morever,

\displaystyle\bar{T}(w^{\prime 2})=\{C_{T}^{*},P_{R}\Omega_{T},F_{T}^{*}\}

(C.38)

where

\displaystyle C_{T}^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h_{a}F^{a}-\frac{1}{2}h_{ab}\bar{\Omega}^{a}\Omega^{b},

(C.39)

\displaystyle P_{R}\Omega_{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\cancel{\mathcal{D}}(-h_{a}\Omega^{a}+h_{\bar{a}}\Omega^{\bar{a}})-h_{\bar{a}b}[(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}X^{b})\Omega^{\bar{a}}-F^{\bar{a}}\Omega^{b}]-\frac{1}{2}h_{\bar{a}\bar{b}c}\Omega^{c}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{a}}\Omega^{\bar{b}}

(C.40)

\displaystyle+h_{a\bar{b}}[(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{X}^{\bar{b}})\Omega^{a}-F^{a}\Omega^{\bar{b}}]+\frac{1}{2}h_{ab\bar{c}}\Omega^{\bar{c}}\bar{\Omega}^{a}\Omega^{b},

\displaystyle F_{T}^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h_{a\bar{b}}\Big(-\mathcal{D}_{\mu}X^{a}\mathcal{D}^{\mu}\bar{X}^{\bar{b}}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Omega}^{a}P_{L}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\Omega^{\bar{b}}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{b}}P_{R}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\Omega^{a}+F^{a}F^{\bar{b}}\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}h_{ab\bar{c}}(-\bar{\Omega}^{a}\Omega^{b}F^{\bar{c}}+\bar{\Omega}^{a}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}X^{b})\Omega^{\bar{c}})+\frac{1}{2}h_{\bar{a}\bar{b}c}(-\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{a}}\Omega^{\bar{b}}F^{c}+\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{a}}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{X}^{\bar{b}})\Omega^{c})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}h_{ab\bar{c}\bar{d}}(\bar{\Omega}^{a}P_{L}\Omega^{b})(\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{c}}P_{R}\Omega^{\bar{d}})+\frac{1}{2}\square^{C}h-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{D}^{\mu}(h_{a}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}X^{a}-h_{\bar{a}}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}\bar{X}^{\bar{a}}+h_{a\bar{b}}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{b}}\gamma_{\mu}\Omega^{a}).

We then present a superconformal composite real multiplet
\mathcal{R}
with Weyl/chiral weights
(0,0)
. Defining some chiral multiplets
\mathcal{X}^{A}\equiv\{X^{A},P_{L}\Omega^{A},F^{A}\}
where
A=\{S_{0},Z^{i},\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda,T(\bar{w}^{\prime 2})
} and their conjugates, we represent the composite one
\mathcal{R}
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}\equiv(S_{0}\bar{S}_{0}e^{-K/3})^{-3}\frac{(\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda)(\bar{\lambda}P_{R}\lambda)}{T(\bar{w}^{\prime 2})\bar{T}(w^{\prime 2})}\mathcal{U}

(C.42)

whose lowest component is

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{R}}\equiv(s_{0}\bar{s}_{0}e^{-K/3})^{-3}\frac{(\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda)(\bar{\lambda}P_{R}\lambda)}{C_{T}C_{\bar{T}}}\mathcal{U}\equiv f(X^{A},\bar{X}^{\bar{A}})

(C.43)

where
C_{T}=-D_{+}^{2}\Delta^{-2}
;
C_{\bar{T}}=-D_{-}^{2}\Delta^{-2}
, and
\Delta\equiv s_{0}\bar{s}_{0}e^{-K/3}
, and
K,\mathcal{U}
are functions of the matter multiplets
Z^{i}
’s.

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{R}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f\equiv(s_{0}\bar{s}_{0}e^{-K/3})^{-3}\frac{(\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda)(\bar{\lambda}P_{R}\lambda)}{C_{T}C_{\bar{T}}}\mathcal{U},

(C.44)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{R}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\sqrt{2}(-f_{A}\Omega^{A}+f_{\bar{A}}\Omega^{\bar{A}}),

(C.45)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{R}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2f_{A}F^{A}+f_{AB}\bar{\Omega}^{A}\Omega^{B},

(C.46)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{\mathcal{R}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2f_{\bar{A}}F^{\bar{A}}+f_{\bar{A}\bar{B}}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{A}}\Omega^{\bar{B}},

(C.47)

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{\mathcal{R}}_{\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle if_{A}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}X^{A}-if_{\bar{A}}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}\bar{X}^{\bar{A}}+if_{A\bar{B}}\bar{\Omega}^{A}\gamma_{\mu}\Omega^{\bar{B}},

(C.48)

\displaystyle P_{L}\Lambda_{\mathcal{R}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{2}if_{\bar{A}B}[(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}X^{B})\Omega^{\bar{A}}-F^{\bar{A}}\Omega^{B}]-\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}f_{\bar{A}\bar{B}C}\Omega^{C}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{A}}\Omega^{\bar{B}},

(C.49)

\displaystyle P_{R}\Lambda_{\mathcal{R}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}if_{A\bar{B}}[(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{X}^{\bar{B}})\Omega^{A}-F^{A}\Omega^{\bar{B}}]+\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}f_{AB\bar{C}}\Omega^{\bar{C}}\bar{\Omega}^{A}\Omega^{B},

(C.50)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{R}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2f_{A\bar{B}}\Big(-\mathcal{D}_{\mu}X^{A}\mathcal{D}^{\mu}\bar{X}^{\bar{B}}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Omega}^{A}P_{L}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\Omega^{\bar{B}}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{B}}P_{R}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\Omega^{A}+F^{A}F^{\bar{B}}\Big)

(C.51)

\displaystyle+f_{AB\bar{C}}(-\bar{\Omega}^{A}\Omega^{B}F^{\bar{C}}+\bar{\Omega}^{A}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}X^{B})\Omega^{\bar{C}})+f_{\bar{A}\bar{B}C}(-\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{A}}\Omega^{\bar{B}}F^{C}+\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{A}}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{X}^{\bar{B}})\Omega^{C})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}f_{AB\bar{C}\bar{D}}(\bar{\Omega}^{A}P_{L}\Omega^{B})(\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{C}}P_{R}\Omega^{\bar{D}}).

Then, the superconformal multiplet of the new Fayet-Iliopoulos term can be written by using

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}\cdot(V)_{D}=\{\tilde{\mathcal{C}},\tilde{\mathcal{Z}},\tilde{\mathcal{H}},\tilde{\mathcal{K}},\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{\mu},\tilde{\Lambda},\tilde{\mathcal{D}}\},

(C.52)

whose superconformal multiplet components are as follows:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{C}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Df,

(C.53)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\lambda+Di\sqrt{2}(-f_{A}\Omega^{A}+f_{\bar{A}}\Omega^{\bar{A}}),

(C.54)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D(-2f_{A}F^{A}+f_{AB}\bar{\Omega}^{A}\Omega^{B})-i\sqrt{2}(-f_{A}\bar{\Omega}^{A}+f_{\bar{A}}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{A}})P_{L}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\lambda,

(C.55)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{K}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D(-2f_{\bar{A}}F^{\bar{A}}+f_{\bar{A}\bar{B}}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{A}}\Omega^{\bar{B}})-i\sqrt{2}(-f_{A}\bar{\Omega}^{A}+f_{\bar{A}}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{A}})P_{R}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\lambda,

(C.56)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{B}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\mathcal{D}^{\nu}\hat{F}_{\mu\nu})f+D(if_{A}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}X^{A}-if_{\bar{A}}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}\bar{X}^{\bar{A}}+if_{A\bar{B}}\bar{\Omega}^{A}\gamma_{\mu}\Omega^{\bar{B}}),

(C.57)

\displaystyle\tilde{\Lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-f\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\lambda+D(P_{L}\Lambda_{\mathcal{R}}+P_{R}\Lambda_{\mathcal{R}})+\frac{1}{2}\Big(\gamma_{*}(-f_{A}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}X^{A}+f_{\bar{A}}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{X}^{\bar{A}}-f_{A\bar{B}}\bar{\Omega}^{A}\cancel{\gamma}\Omega^{\bar{B}})

(C.58)

\displaystyle+P_{L}(-2f_{\bar{A}}F^{\bar{A}}+f_{\bar{A}\bar{B}}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{A}}\Omega^{\bar{B}})+P_{R}(-2f_{A}F^{A}+f_{AB}\bar{\Omega}^{A}\Omega^{B})-\cancel{\mathcal{D}}f\Big)\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\lambda

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\Big(i\gamma_{*}\gamma^{\mu}\mathcal{D}^{\nu}\hat{F}_{\mu\nu}-\cancel{\mathcal{D}}D\Big)i\sqrt{2}(-f_{A}\Omega^{A}+f_{\bar{A}}\Omega^{\bar{A}}),

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{D}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-f\square^{C}D+D\bigg\{2f_{A\bar{B}}(-\mathcal{D}_{\mu}X^{A}\mathcal{D}^{\mu}\bar{X}^{\bar{B}}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Omega}^{A}P_{L}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\Omega^{\bar{B}}-\frac{1}{2}\cancel{\Omega}^{\bar{B}}P_{R}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\Omega^{A}+F^{A}F^{\bar{B}})

(C.59)

\displaystyle+f_{AB\bar{C}}(-\bar{\Omega}^{A}\Omega^{B}F^{\bar{C}}+\bar{\Omega}^{A}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}X^{B})\Omega^{\bar{C}})+f_{\bar{A}\bar{B}C}(-\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{A}}\Omega^{\bar{B}}F^{C}+\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{A}}(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{X}^{\bar{B}})\Omega^{C})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}f_{AB\bar{C}\bar{D}}(\bar{\Omega}^{A}P_{L}\Omega^{B})(\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{C}}P_{R}\Omega^{\bar{D}})\bigg\}

\displaystyle-(\mathcal{D}_{\nu}\hat{F}^{\mu\nu})(if_{A}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}X^{A}-if_{\bar{A}}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}\bar{X}^{\bar{A}}+if_{A\bar{B}}\bar{\Omega}^{A}\gamma_{\mu}\Omega^{\bar{B}})

\displaystyle+\bigg(\sqrt{2}if_{\bar{A}B}[(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}X^{B})\Omega^{\bar{A}}-F^{\bar{A}}\Omega^{B}]+\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}f_{\bar{A}\bar{B}C}\Omega^{C}\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{A}}\Omega^{\bar{B}}\bigg)\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\lambda

\displaystyle-\bigg(\sqrt{2}if_{A\bar{B}}[(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\bar{X}^{\bar{B}})\Omega^{A}-F^{A}\Omega^{\bar{B}}]+\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}f_{AB\bar{C}}\Omega^{\bar{C}}\bar{\Omega}^{A}\Omega^{B}\bigg)\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\lambda

\displaystyle-(\mathcal{D}_{\mu}f)(\mathcal{D}^{\mu}D)-\frac{1}{2}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}[i\sqrt{2}(-f_{A}\Omega^{A}+f_{\bar{A}}\Omega^{\bar{A}})](\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\lambda)+\frac{1}{2}i\sqrt{2}(-f_{A}\Omega^{A}+f_{\bar{A}}\Omega^{\bar{A}})(\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\lambda),

where the indices
A,B,C,D
run over
0,i,W,T
. The component action of the new FI term is then given by the D-term density formula

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{NEW}\equiv-[\mathcal{R}\cdot(V)_{D}]_{D}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\int d^{4}xe\bigg[\tilde{\mathcal{D}}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\psi}\cdot\gamma i\gamma_{*}\tilde{\Lambda}-\frac{1}{3}\tilde{\mathcal{C}}R(\omega)

(C.60)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{6}\Big(\tilde{\mathcal{C}}\bar{\psi}_{\mu}\gamma^{\mu\rho\sigma}-i\bar{\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}}\gamma^{\rho\sigma}\gamma_{*}\Big)R^{\prime}_{\rho\sigma}(Q)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}\varepsilon^{abcd}\bar{\psi}_{a}\gamma_{b}\psi_{c}\Big(\tilde{\mathcal{B}}_{d}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\psi}_{d}\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}\Big)\bigg]+\textrm{h.c.}.

Using
f=\Delta^{-3}\frac{W\bar{W}}{C_{T}C_{\bar{T}}}\mathcal{U}
,
W\equiv(\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda)
,
\bar{W}\equiv(\bar{\lambda}P_{R}\lambda)
,
\Omega^{W}\sim\sqrt{2}iDP_{L}\lambda
,
\bar{\Omega}^{T}\sim 2D^{2}\Delta^{-2}(\frac{\bar{\Omega}^{0}}{s_{0}}-\frac{K_{I}\bar{\Omega}^{I}}{3})
,
C_{T}\sim-D^{2}\Delta^{-2}
,
F^{W}\sim-D^{2}
,
F^{\bar{T}}\sim 2D^{2}\Delta^{-2}(\frac{F^{\bar{0}}}{\bar{s}_{0}}-\frac{1}{3}K_{\bar{J}}F^{\bar{J}})
where
\Delta\equiv s_{0}\bar{s}_{0}e^{-K/3}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\textrm{newFI}}^{(\textrm{2f})}e^{-1}

\displaystyle=-Df_{0\bar{W}}F^{0}F^{\bar{W}}-Df_{I\bar{W}}F^{I}F^{\bar{W}}-Df_{T\bar{W}}F^{T}F^{\bar{W}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}f_{0W\bar{W}}\bar{\Omega}^{0}\Omega^{W}F^{\bar{W}}+\frac{1}{2}f_{IW\bar{W}}\bar{\Omega}^{I}\Omega^{W}F^{\bar{W}}+\frac{1}{2}f_{TW\bar{W}}\bar{\Omega}^{T}\Omega^{W}F^{\bar{W}}-\frac{D\sqrt{2}}{4}\bar{\psi}_{\mu}\gamma^{\mu}f_{\bar{W}W}F^{\bar{W}}\Omega^{W}+c.c..,

\displaystyle=-3\Delta\frac{(\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda)}{D}\mathcal{U}\frac{F^{0}}{s_{0}}+\Delta\frac{(\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda)}{D}(K_{I}\mathcal{U}+\mathcal{U}_{I})F^{I}+2\Delta\frac{(\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda)}{D}\mathcal{U}(\frac{F^{0}}{s_{0}}-\frac{1}{3}K_{I}F^{I})

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{3i}{\sqrt{2}}\frac{\Delta}{Ds_{0}}\mathcal{U}(\bar{\Omega}^{0}P_{L}\lambda)-\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}\frac{\Delta}{D}(K_{I}\mathcal{U}+\mathcal{U}_{I})(\bar{\Omega}^{I}P_{L}\lambda)+\sqrt{2}i\frac{\Delta}{Ds_{0}}\mathcal{U}(\bar{\Omega}^{0}P_{L}\lambda)-\frac{\sqrt{2}i}{3}\frac{\Delta}{D}\mathcal{U}K_{I}(\bar{\Omega}^{I}P_{L}\lambda)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{i}{2}\Delta\mathcal{U}(\bar{\psi}_{\mu}\gamma^{\mu}P_{L}\lambda)+c.c.,

\displaystyle=\frac{\Delta}{D}\bigg(-\frac{F^{0}\mathcal{U}}{s_{0}}+\mathcal{U}_{I}F^{I}+\frac{1}{3}\mathcal{U}K_{I}F^{I}\bigg)(\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda)+\frac{5}{\sqrt{2}}i\frac{\Delta}{Ds_{0}}\mathcal{U}(\bar{\Omega}^{0}P_{L}\lambda)

\displaystyle\quad-i\frac{\Delta}{D}\bigg(\frac{5}{3\sqrt{2}}K_{I}\mathcal{U}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathcal{U}_{I}\bigg)(\bar{\Omega}^{I}P_{L}\lambda)+\frac{i}{2}\Delta\mathcal{U}(\bar{\psi}_{\mu}\gamma^{\mu}P_{L}\lambda)+h.c.

(C.61)

At the superconformal gauge (i.e.
P_{L}\Omega^{0}=\frac{1}{3}e^{K/6}K_{I}P_{L}\Omega^{I}
,
s_{0}=\bar{s}_{0}=e^{K/6}
,
\Delta=1
), the lagrangian is rewritten by

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\textrm{newFI}}^{(\textrm{2f})}e^{-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{D}\bigg(-F^{0}\mathcal{U}e^{-K/6}+\mathcal{U}_{I}F^{I}+\frac{1}{3}\mathcal{U}K_{I}F^{I}\bigg)(\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda)-\frac{i}{D}\frac{\mathcal{U}_{I}}{\sqrt{2}}(\bar{\Omega}^{I}P_{L}\lambda)+\frac{i}{2}\mathcal{U}(\bar{\psi}_{\mu}\gamma^{\mu}P_{L}\lambda)+h.c.

The D-term lagrangian is found to be

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{D}e^{-1}

\displaystyle\supset

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}D^{2}-\mathcal{U}D+\frac{1}{D}\bigg(-F^{0}\mathcal{U}e^{-K/6}+\mathcal{U}_{I}F^{I}+\frac{1}{3}\mathcal{U}K_{I}F^{I}\bigg)(\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda)-\frac{i}{D}\frac{\mathcal{U}_{I}}{\sqrt{2}}(\bar{\Omega}^{I}P_{L}\lambda)

(C.63)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{D}\bigg(-F^{\bar{0}}\mathcal{U}e^{-K/6}+\mathcal{U}_{\bar{J}}F^{\bar{J}}+\frac{1}{3}\mathcal{U}K_{\bar{J}}F^{\bar{J}}\bigg)(\bar{\lambda}P_{R}\lambda)+\frac{i}{D}\frac{\mathcal{U}_{\bar{J}}}{\sqrt{2}}(\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{J}}P_{R}\lambda)

The solution for
D
can be obtained by

\displaystyle D=\mathcal{U}+\frac{1}{\mathcal{U}^{2}}\bigg[\bigg(-F^{0}\mathcal{U}e^{-K/6}+\mathcal{U}_{I}F^{I}+\frac{1}{3}\mathcal{U}K_{I}F^{I}\bigg)(\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda)-i\frac{\mathcal{U}_{I}}{\sqrt{2}}(\bar{\Omega}^{I}P_{L}\lambda)+h.c.\bigg]+\textrm{higher order terms},

Then, we find

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\textrm{newFI}}^{(2f)}e^{-1}=\frac{1}{\mathcal{U}}\bigg[\bigg(-F^{0}\mathcal{U}e^{-K/6}+\mathcal{U}_{I}F^{I}+\frac{1}{3}\mathcal{U}K_{I}F^{I}\bigg)(\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda)-i\frac{\mathcal{U}_{I}}{\sqrt{2}}(\bar{\Omega}^{I}P_{L}\lambda)+h.c.\bigg]

(C.65)

The total lagrangian containing the auxiliary fields
F^{0}
and
F^{I}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}e^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-3e^{-K/3}F^{0}F^{\bar{0}}+3e^{K/3}WF^{0}+3e^{K/3}\bar{W}F^{\bar{0}}+\frac{1}{9}G_{I\bar{J}}F^{I}F^{\bar{J}}

(C.66)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{3}e^{K/2}\nabla_{I}WF^{I}+\frac{1}{3}e^{K/2}\nabla_{\bar{J}}\bar{W}F^{\bar{J}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\mathcal{U}}\bigg[\bigg(-F^{0}\mathcal{U}e^{-K/6}+\mathcal{U}_{I}F^{I}+\frac{1}{3}\mathcal{U}K_{I}F^{I}\bigg)(\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda)-i\frac{\mathcal{U}_{I}}{\sqrt{2}}(\bar{\Omega}^{I}P_{L}\lambda)\bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\mathcal{U}}\bigg[\bigg(-F^{\bar{0}}\mathcal{U}e^{-K/6}+\mathcal{U}_{\bar{J}}F^{\bar{J}}+\frac{1}{3}\mathcal{U}K_{\bar{J}}F^{\bar{J}}\bigg)(\bar{\lambda}P_{R}\lambda)+i\frac{\mathcal{U}_{\bar{J}}}{\sqrt{2}}(\bar{\Omega}^{\bar{J}}P_{R}\lambda)\bigg],

where
\nabla_{I}W\equiv W_{I}+K_{I}W
. By solving the equations of motion for the auxiliary fields, we find

\displaystyle F^{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{2K/3}\bar{W}-\frac{1}{3}e^{K/6}(\bar{\lambda}P_{R}\lambda),

(C.67)

\displaystyle F^{\bar{J}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-3e^{K/2}G^{I\bar{J}}\nabla_{I}W-G^{I\bar{J}}\Big(9\frac{\mathcal{U}_{I}}{\mathcal{U}}+3K_{I}\Big)(\bar{\lambda}P_{L}\lambda)

(C.68)

and also read the mass
m_{I\lambda}

\displaystyle m_{I\lambda}^{FI}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}\frac{\mathcal{U}_{I}}{\mathcal{U}},

(C.69)

\displaystyle m_{\lambda\lambda}^{FI}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-e^{K/2}\left(\bar{W}+4G^{I\bar{J}}\left(\frac{\mathcal{U}_{I}}{\mathcal{U}}+\frac{K_{I}}{3}\right)(\bar{W}_{\bar{J}}+K_{\bar{J}}\bar{W})\right)

(C.70)

The gravitino mixing term is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\textrm{mix}}e^{-1}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\nabla_{I}We^{K/2}\bar{\psi}_{\mu}\gamma^{\mu}P_{L}\Omega^{I}+\frac{i}{2}\mathcal{P}_{A}\bar{\psi}_{\mu}\gamma^{\mu}P_{L}\lambda^{A}+\frac{i}{2}\mathcal{U}\bar{\psi}_{\mu}\gamma^{\mu}P_{L}\lambda+h.c=-\bar{\psi}_{\mu}\gamma^{\mu}P_{L}v+h.c.,

(C.71)

which gives the goldstino

\displaystyle P_{L}v=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\nabla_{I}We^{K/2}P_{L}\Omega^{I}-\frac{i}{2}\mathcal{P}_{A}P_{L}\lambda^{A}-\frac{i}{2}\mathcal{U}P_{L}\lambda,

(C.72)

where
\lambda^{A}
is the gaugino corresponding to the gauge multiplet
V_{A}
, and
\lambda
is the superpartner of the new FI term vector multiplet
V
.

The fermionic masses from standard
\mathcal{N}=1
supergravity are found by

\displaystyle m_{3/2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{K/2}W,

(C.73)

\displaystyle m_{IJ}^{(0)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{K/2}(\partial_{I}+K_{I})(W_{J}+K_{J}W)-e^{K/2}G^{K\bar{L}}\partial_{I}G_{J\bar{L}}(W_{K}+K_{K}W),

(C.74)

\displaystyle m_{IA}^{(0)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\sqrt{2}[\partial_{I}\mathcal{P}_{A}-\frac{1}{4}f_{ABI}(\textrm{Re}f)^{-1~BC}\mathcal{P}_{C}],

(C.75)

\displaystyle m_{AB}^{(0)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}e^{K/2}f_{ABI}G^{I\bar{J}}(\bar{W}_{\bar{J}}+K_{\bar{J}}\bar{W}),

(C.76)

\displaystyle m_{I\lambda}^{(0)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0,

(C.77)

\displaystyle m_{\lambda\lambda}^{(0)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.

(C.78)

The fermionic masses from super-Higgs effect are given by

\displaystyle m_{IJ}^{(\nu)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{3W}e^{K/2}(W_{I}+K_{I}W)(W_{J}+K_{J}W),

(C.79)

\displaystyle m_{IA}^{(\nu)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i\frac{2}{3\sqrt{2}W}(W_{I}+K_{I}W)\mathcal{P}_{A},

(C.80)

\displaystyle m_{AB}^{(\nu)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3e^{K/2}W}\mathcal{P}_{A}\mathcal{P}_{B},

(C.81)

\displaystyle m_{I\lambda}^{(\nu)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i\frac{2}{3\sqrt{2}W}(W_{I}+K_{I}W)\mathcal{U},

(C.82)

\displaystyle m_{\lambda\lambda}^{(\nu)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathcal{U}^{2}}{3e^{K/2}W}

(C.83)

The fermionic masses from the new FI term are found to be

\displaystyle m_{IJ}^{FI}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0,

(C.84)

\displaystyle m_{IA}^{FI}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0,

(C.85)

\displaystyle m_{AB}^{FI}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0,

(C.86)

\displaystyle m_{I\lambda}^{FI}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}\frac{\mathcal{U}_{I}}{\mathcal{U}},

(C.87)

\displaystyle m_{\lambda\lambda}^{FI}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-e^{K/2}\left(\bar{W}+4G^{I\bar{J}}\left(\frac{\mathcal{U}_{I}}{\mathcal{U}}+\frac{K_{I}}{3}\right)(\bar{W}_{\bar{J}}+K_{\bar{J}}\bar{W})\right).

(C.88)

Thus, the final fermionic masses are made by combinations of the three contributions above as follows:

\displaystyle m_{3/2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle We^{K/2},

\displaystyle m_{IJ}^{(g)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m_{IJ}^{(0)}+m_{IJ}^{FI}+m_{IJ}^{(\nu)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{K/2}(W_{IJ}+K_{IJ}W+K_{J}W_{I}+K_{I}W_{J}+K_{I}K_{J}W)

\displaystyle-e^{K/2}G^{K\bar{L}}\partial_{I}G_{J\bar{L}}(W_{K}+K_{K}W)-\frac{2}{3}(W_{I}+K_{I}W)(W_{J}+K_{J}W),

\displaystyle m_{IA}^{(g)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m_{IA}^{(0)}+m_{IA}^{FI}+m_{IA}^{(\nu)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\sqrt{2}[\partial_{I}\mathcal{P}_{A}-\frac{1}{4}f_{ABI}(\textrm{Re}f)^{-1~BC}\mathcal{P}_{C}]-i\frac{2}{3\sqrt{2}W}(W_{I}+K_{I}W)\mathcal{P}_{A}

\displaystyle m_{AB}^{(g)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m_{IA}^{(0)}+m_{IA}^{FI}+m_{IA}^{(\nu)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}e^{K/2}f_{ABI}G^{I\bar{J}}(\bar{W}_{\bar{J}}+K_{\bar{J}}\bar{W})+\frac{1}{3e^{K/2}W}\mathcal{P}_{A}\mathcal{P}_{B}

\displaystyle m_{I\lambda}^{(g)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m_{I\lambda}^{(0)}+m_{I\lambda}^{FI}+m_{I\lambda}^{(\nu)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{i}{\sqrt{2}}\frac{\mathcal{U}_{I}}{\mathcal{U}}-\frac{i\sqrt{2}}{3W}(W_{I}+K_{I}W)\mathcal{U}=m_{\lambda I}^{(g)},

\displaystyle m_{\lambda\lambda}^{(g)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m_{\lambda\lambda}^{(0)}+m_{\lambda\lambda}^{FI}+m_{\lambda\lambda}^{(\nu)}

(C.89)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-e^{K/2}\left(\bar{W}+4G^{I\bar{J}}\left(\frac{\mathcal{U}_{I}}{\mathcal{U}}+\frac{K_{I}}{3}\right)(\bar{W}_{\bar{J}}+K_{\bar{J}}\bar{W})\right)+\frac{\mathcal{U}^{2}}{3e^{K/2}W}.

Appendix D
Scanning Nonrenormalizable Terms of New FI Terms

The selection rules we defined are

\displaystyle\textrm{For }(d,f,a),

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{C}}:\quad(0,0,0)\implies\Gamma(\tilde{\mathcal{C}})=0\qquad\delta_{add}=2,4,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}:\quad{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}(0,1,0)}\implies\Gamma(\tilde{\mathcal{Z}})=1\qquad\delta_{add}=5/2,~9/2,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{B}_{a}}:\quad{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}(1,0,0)},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(0,2,0)}\implies\Gamma(\tilde{\mathcal{B}_{a}})=1,2\qquad\delta_{add}=3,

\displaystyle\tilde{\Lambda}:\quad{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}(1,1,0)},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}(1,1,1)},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(0,3,0)}\implies\Gamma(\tilde{\Lambda})=2,3\qquad\delta_{add}=3/2,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{D}}:\quad(0,0,0),{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}(2,0,0)},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(0,2,0)},{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}(0,0,2)},

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(0,2,1)},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(1,2,0)},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}(0,4,0)}\implies\Gamma(\tilde{\mathcal{D}})=0,2,3,4\qquad\delta_{add}=0.

and

term action of the new FI term, i.e.
\mathcal{L}_{newFI}e^{-1}\supset\frac{D}{4}f_{W\bar{T}}\bar{\Omega}^{W}\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\Omega^{\bar{T}}\sim D^{-2}\mathcal{O}^{(7)}_{4\lambda}
.

Case of
b=2
: This case corresponds to the fermionic kinetic term in the
\tilde{\mathcal{D}}
of the supergravity action. Thus, all the terms from this case vanish on-shell (i.e.
\mathcal{D}_{a}F^{ab}=0,\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\lambda=0
and using Bianchi identities
\mathcal{D}_{[a}F_{b]c}=0
) because
\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\Omega^{W}=\cancel{\mathcal{D}}\Big(\sqrt{2}P_{L}(-\frac{1}{2}\gamma\cdot\hat{F}+iD)\lambda\Big)=0
on-shell and
\cancel{\mathcal{D}}D\sim\mathcal{U}^{(1)}D\sim 0
along the potential minima.

\displaystyle 3a_{T2}-1,

(D.75)

which gives
\alpha=0
and renormalizable terms.

\displaystyle(N-u_{2})_{b=2,f_{W1}^{\lambda}=f_{W2}^{\lambda}=1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-a_{s1}-a_{z1}-a_{T1},

(D.76)

\displaystyle(\delta_{tot}-4)_{b=2,f_{W1}^{\lambda}=f_{W2}^{\lambda}=1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 3a_{T2}+1.

(D.77)

which gives
\alpha=0,1/4
.
